**On Taiwan's own two-year moves, on the same months as the shares** (as 8j's real-currency test: the history model's
pots and contribution, rates at today's forwards; the move measured from the meetings, 36 months after VT is bought,
to the gift, 60 months; 366 windows, VT bought Jan 1988 to Jun 2018, so meetings Jan 1991 to Jun 2021):
- The actual joint move (meetings since 1991): 1-in-20 low -12.8%, median +3.2%, 1-in-20 high +28.1%, largest +38.9% (VT bought Jul 2003);
  above 28% in 5.5% of windows. The contribution: $105,000 / $115,000 / $130,000 (median was $120,000).
- **Her flexibility plus anything above the top covered the actual move in 91.5% of windows** (fails in 31; median gap $6,416, was $5,408; largest
  $15,334, was $14,629, VT bought Jul 2003, was Aug 2003). **The half kept alone: 82.0%** (fails in 66; was 82.5%, 64;
  largest gap $24,350, was $24,188, Jul 2003). On the stated bottom: 94.5% (was 95.1%) and 88.8% (the lower reading,
  the pot alone; Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner: not re-run with the gap between the ends, kept out of prose).
- **Where it fails (with the excess), four episodes** (failing VT windows; not card Q210's four episodes of joint rises of 28% or more, which are in 8x; note added Sun 22 Nov evening, second-way check): VT bought Jan 1988 (meetings 1991, the 1992 gravel crisis);
  most months from Feb 1999 to Aug 2000 (meetings 2002-03: the 2002-04 cost burst came after the 2000-02 share fall, so the half kept
  bought near the peak was small); every month Feb to Sep 2003 (meetings 2006, the 2006-08 burst; was Mar to Oct); Mar, Apr and Jun to Aug 2006
  (meetings 2009, the Taiwan dollar's rebound after the crash; was Mar and May to Aug).
- **By era:** meetings by Dec 2000, covered in 99.2% (1 fail in 120); meetings from Jan 2001, 87.8% (30 in 246).
  January purchases only (Laura's calendar): 29 of 31 (fails 1988 and 2000).
- **The worst window itself** (VT bought Jul 2003; meetings Jul 2006, gift Jul 2008): contribution $115,000, need
  $44,756, half kept $20,406 (was $20,568) plus excess $9,016 (was $10,000) = $29,422 (was $30,569), **$15,334 short**
  (was $14,187); since the re-size it is also the worst window with the excess (Aug 2003's $14,629 before). Other named
  windows: 1991-93 (VT Jan 1988) $1,428 short of a $37,254 need (was $191); 2002-04 (VT Jan 1999) $2,231 left (was
  $3,280); 2009-11 (VT Mar 2006) $1,810 short (was $785); 2019-21 (VT Aug 2016; contribution now $115,000, was
  $120,000; need $33,380, was $34,832) $10,085 left (was $4,892).
- **Drawn apart** (every house path's pot against every 24-month move since 1991): her flexibility plus anything above the top covers 90.6% (half
  kept alone 82.4%; was 90.7% and 82.6%); history pots 92.6% (85.2%); joint replay pots 93.0% (85.5%; was 93.1%, 85.6%).

What it shows:
- **The 1-in-20 joint rise since 1991 is about all of her flexibility plus anything above the top.** (Label corrected Thu 19 Nov: was "her whole flexibility".) At the centre, 28% on $115,000 needs $32,191, $3,509 more
  than $28,682 (was $2,368 more than $29,823; card Q10e's own figures, now on the register's paths). Across the house model that pot covers it
  in about 1 path in 3; history and the joint replay, about 2 in 5 and 1 in 2. The half kept alone covers it in about 1
  path in 9 (house) to 1 in 7 (history and the joint replay).
- **That is a 1-in-20 rise put on every path, so it is harsher than any one history.** On Taiwan's own moves since
  1991, matched to the same months' shares, that pot covered the move in about 9 windows in 10 (91.5%), and in
  about 9 in 10 when house pots meet history's moves drawn apart (90.6%).
- **The worst two years since 1991 needed about one and a half times her central flexibility plus anything above the top** ($44,756 against $28,682). Replayed
  on its own months the plan would have been $15,334 short (was $14,187). It is covered in about 1 house path in 19.
- **On the stated bottom** the 1-in-20 rise is covered centrally with $2,090 to spare (was $3,230), and in 57.7% of house paths.
  (Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner; bond desk 5, L5: that is the lower reading, the pot alone; with the $20,000
  between the ends, which a budget set on the bottom also has, $22,090 to spare and 96.7% of house paths. Neither is for
  prose: co-sponsors count the top.)
- **18 months instead of 24** (meetings, and so the NT$ budget, in mid-2031): the 1-in-20 rise is +24.6% and her
  central flexibility plus anything above the top covers it with $407 left (was $1,547); house 51.0%. (The 18-month 1-in-20 is since 1991.)
- **The link with shares runs the other way over five years.** On this history model (US stocks), the joint move rose
  with shares over the same two years since 2001 (+0.28; the Taiwan dollar part +0.40, close to card Q10e's +0.32 on US
  stocks and +0.42 on world shares). But the half kept is VT over five years, and against the five-year share return the
  joint move runs -0.30 since 1988 and -0.15 for meetings since 2001 (the Taiwan dollar part alone -0.28 and -0.12).
  That is why coverage since 2001 (87.8%) is lower than before (99.2%). Card Q10e's rough replay (3.6% short, world
  shares, windows ending from Dec 2005) started after the 2002-04 burst.
- **For the lead (register wording, record only):** 8f's last bullet and 8j's correction (both Sun 15 Nov, from card
  Q10e) say that since 2001 a stronger Taiwan dollar "has come with rising shares, so the half kept has tended to be
  large in those years". The first half holds over the same two years. The second does not on this register's history
  model: the half kept is set by five years of shares, and its link with the Taiwan dollar's move since 2001 is -0.12
  (US stocks). A safer note: "a stronger Taiwan dollar has come with rising shares in the same two years, not with a
  larger half kept".
- **Nothing in the plan changes.** 8j's 10% stays as the currency test (Table 3 row 7). The 28% is two of IPS l.15's
  three uses together; the third, a co-sponsor falling short, is not in it. The case asks for no contingency sizing
  (CANON A31): these figures show how fast two uses can draw on her flexibility, never a facility cost estimate. Never
  "a 10% joint stress".
- **Limits:** the national index is not her building (card Q10c claim 3); monthly averages; about 16 to 17 independent
  two-year spans since 1991, so each history share describes the past, not odds for 2031; US stocks stand in for VT;
  rates at today's forwards in the history model; the build after the dollars are converted is not counted (card Q10e
  row 32: +31.8% at 1 in 20 with a 12-month build); the half kept is a market value on 1 Jan 2033.

**Added Wed 18 Nov evening (card Q131)** (numbers owner; Keel arithmetic on data read at source, not MODEL;
`numbers/strong_twd_windows.py`, `numbers/results_strong_twd_windows_2026-11-18.json`; no figure above is changed)
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/strong_twd_windows.py` from the repo root (under a
  second). It runs no model and imports no register module: it parses card Q10e's index out of `taiwan_joint.py`
  (CCI_TEXT, never executed), reads the EXTAUS mirror and card Q131's monthly file, takes 8q's central pots and 1-in-20
  from `results_taiwan_joint_2026-11-17.json`, and writes only its own results file. Every figure in this block is asserted.
- **Data.** DGBAS's construction cost index, Jan 1991 to Sep 2026, as card Q10e read it (8q's own series), plus October
  2026 at 120.50 (card Q103, read at source; 8d); NT$ per US$ from the FRED EXTAUS mirror (verified, 8l) plus October
  2026 at 32.2695 (card Q131's FRED read, preliminary). Card Q131's own file (a separate read of DGBAS's database)
  agrees with both in all 430 months, to the last digit. On 8q's data (405 windows) the series gives 8q's 1-in-20,
  +28.0%, exactly; with October added (406 windows) it is still 28.0%. No strong window needs October: the same 30
  windows come out of 8q's data to Sep 2026.
- **The windows (card Q131's definition; 8j's and 8q's conventions).**
  - A window runs 24 months, from one month's average to the same month two years later. Start months run Jan 1991 to
    Oct 2024: 406 windows.
  - The Taiwan dollar's move = NT$ per US$ at the start / NT$ per US$ at the end - 1: the % more US dollars a fixed NT$
    bill needs at the end. "Strong" means 10% or more, 8j's test size.
  - The rise in the US-dollar cost of a fixed NT$ building budget = (index at the end / index at the start) x (NT$ per
    US$ at the start / at the end) - 1. This is 8q's joint move.
  - **30 of the 406 windows since 1991 (7.4%) are strong.** They are also the 30 largest Taiwan dollar moves: the closest to the
    line, Jan 2019 to Jan 2021, is 10.00%, and the next below it is 9.95%.
  - They fall in 7 runs of start months (card Q131's 7 episodes), which make 5 periods that share no month: 1998-2000
    (5 windows), 2003-05 (5), 2008-11 (9), 2016-18 (4) and 2019-21 (7).
- **Re-derived: the card's figures hold.**
  - In the 30 windows since 1991 the US-dollar cost rose a **median 22.3%** (22.27%). The **lowest was 9.3%** (Sep 1998
    to Sep 2000) and the **highest 29.0%** (Mar 2003 to Mar 2005; Aug 2019 to Aug 2021 is also 29.03%). **Label added
    Sun 22 Nov evening (numbers owner; card Q217's rater; 8x):** 29.0% is the highest of the 30 strong-Taiwan-dollar
    windows only. It is 11th of all 406 windows since 1991: 10 rose more, every one with the Taiwan dollar less than 10%
    stronger, and **the largest joint rise since 1991 is 8q's +38.9%** (Jul 2006 to Jul 2008). Never "the highest joint
    rise since 1991" for 29.0%; the range is "9.3% to 29.0% in the 30 strong windows since 1991".
  - It **beat the currency move in 25 of 30**. The five that did not are the 1998-2000 windows, where the index fell
    0.7% to 1.6% in NT$.
  - The currency itself moved a median 11.7%, and the index in NT$ a median 7.2%, so the median US-dollar rise is 1.9
    times the median currency move (card Q131: "about twice"). In the other 376 windows the median rise was 2.0%.
- **Against 8q's central figures** (Keel arithmetic, as 8q's central column: need = the rise times the figure; left =
  the pot less the need, at face; the pots are 8q's central ones, $28,682 = the half kept $21,179 plus the $7,503 above
  the top, which WORDS calls "her flexibility plus anything above the top"):

| Rise in the US-dollar cost of the building (strong windows since 1991; 8q's 1-in-20 since 1991) | Need on the $115,000 top | Left: half kept plus the excess ($28,682) | Left: half kept alone ($21,179) | On the $95,000 bottom, the lower reading (pot alone; Fri 20 Nov: add $20,000 for the gap between the ends; never in prose): need; left (half kept plus the excess) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Lowest strong window, +9.3% | $10,701 | $17,981 | $10,478 | $8,840; $19,842 |
| **Median strong window, +22.3%** | **$25,607** | **$3,075 left** | $4,428 short | $21,154; $7,528 (half kept alone: $25 left) |
| 8q's 1-in-20 of all two-year spans since 1991, +28.0% (for comparison) | $32,191 | $3,509 short | $11,012 short | $26,593; $2,090 |
| **Highest strong window, +29.0%** | **$33,386** | **$4,704 short** | $12,207 short | $27,580; $1,102 |

  - **Card Q131 row 40 reproduces.** Its "about $25,600" and "about $33,400" are right to the nearest $100. Its "near
    8q's +28.0% row ($3,509 short)" is $1,195 more need than that row: $4,704 short.
  - **Against 8q's central cover (24.9%).** The median strong window is inside it, with $3,075 to spare. 10 of the 30
    windows pass it, all from two periods: all 5 of 2003-05 and 5 of the 7 of 2019-21. The highest in 2008-11 is 24.6%,
    just inside.
  - **Against 8q's 1-in-20 (28.0%).** 7 of the 30 pass it (4 from 2003-05, 3 from 2019-21). Read the other way: of the
    21 spans above 28.0% in 8q's 405 since 1991, 7 are strong-Taiwan-dollar windows. The other 14 are building-cost bursts with the
    Taiwan dollar only 2.2% to 8.6% stronger (starts in 1991, 2002-03 and 2006; the worst, 38.9%, came with 7.2%). So a
    10% stronger Taiwan dollar does not mark the worst spans; it marks spans in which the same building cost about 22%
    more in US dollars.
  - **Against the half kept alone (18.4% centrally).** 21 of the 30 windows pass it.
  - **Beside 8q's three worlds** (read, not run). The median, 22.3%, lies above the rise each world covers 19 times in
    20 (15.8% house, 18.0% history, 17.6% joint replay) and below 8q's +24.6% row (covered in 51.0%, 64.9% and 65.3% of
    paths). These windows were not run through the three worlds' paths; 8q's tables stay the register's test.
- **Robustness (Keel arithmetic).**
  - Weighting each period once instead of each window, the five period medians are 9.9%, 18.6%, 22.3%, 27.0% and
    28.5%. Their median is again 22.3%.
  - Moving the line: at 9% stronger, 39 windows and a median of 21.2%. At 9.5%, 10.5% or 11%: 32, 24 or 20 windows, each
    with a median of 22.4%. The lowest stays 9.1% to 9.3% and the highest 29.0%.
  - January starts only (Laura's calendar, as 8q's "January purchases only"): 4 of the 30 (1998, 2009, 2016, 2019), at
    +9.7%, +19.3%, +19.5% and +18.8%. None passes 24.9%.
- **Caveats.**
  - **Past episodes, not odds.** The windows are chosen on the currency. 7.4% is how often two-year spans since 1991
    had a Taiwan dollar 10% stronger; it is not a chance for 2031-33, and 22.3% is a past median, never a forecast.
  - **Overlap.** 30 overlapping windows are 5 periods, and 2008-11 alone gives 9 of the 30 and holds the median window.
    Counting each period once gives the same median (above), but five periods is a small sample (8q: about 16 to 17
    independent two-year spans since 1991).
  - **The dollar-commodity link (card Q131 row 31).** BIS reads the "standard pattern" (a weaker US dollar with dearer
    commodities) as broken in 2021-22, partly for a lasting reason; industrial metals kept the link best. Every one of
    the 30 windows ended by Sep 2021, before the break. Since 2022 the only 12-month window with the Taiwan dollar 10%
    or more stronger (Jul 2024 to Jul 2025, 11.3%) came with world steel falling, and the US-dollar cost rose 11.1%,
    about the currency alone (card Q131 row 22). If the link stays weak, a stronger Taiwan dollar would land nearer
    8j's 10% than the median 22%; if it returns, the record above applies.
  - **As 8q.** The national index is not her building; monthly averages; the build after the dollars are converted is
    not counted. October 2026's index is revisable for three months and FRED's October rate is preliminary; neither
    enters any strong window.
- **What it adds (record only; nothing in the plan changes).** 8q asks how large a rise her flexibility plus the excess
  covers; this asks what a 10% stronger Taiwan dollar has come with. In Taiwan's record since 1991 it came with a
  dearer NT$ building, a median 22.3% in US dollars against a median currency move of 11.7%: at the median, inside the
  $28,682 and past the half kept alone; in 10 of the 30 windows (2003-05 and 2019-21), past both. 8j's 10% test ($11,500) stays the
  currency-alone test (Table 3 row 7); card Q131 row 41's joint watch is for Table 3's owner.

### 8r. The move date inside 2031: 1 January, 1 April or 1 July (Mon 16 Nov, numbers owner, MODEL; `numbers/move_date.py`, `numbers/results_move_date_2026-11-17.json`; re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening: 0.5% margin on the payments, 365.25-day fix, the Mon 16 Nov file `results_move_date_2026-11-16.json` is the record and its figures are in brackets)
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/move_date.py` from the repo root (about 8 seconds). Imports
  engine, plan and completion; changes no other script, reads no results file and writes no other file.
- **The question.** IPS l.15: "Before you meet co-sponsors in 2031, the other half will move into Treasuries." The
  register moves it on **1 Jan 2031** (section 4: the trade on Thu 2 Jan 2031), and that stays the base. This runs the
  same plan with the move on 1 Apr and 1 Jul 2031.
- **What the date changes, and nothing else.**
  - VT grows 3, 3.25 or 3.5 years before half of it moves.
  - The moved half buys the late-2032 fund at that day's yield: 4.83%, 4.85% or 4.87% forward on the 6 Nov curve, plus
    the path's yield move.
  - The top is the range rule on that day: completion.zero_per_dollar on the move day, less 0.5%, rounded down once.
  - **The bottom (fixed January 2028) and the half kept (VT on 1 Jan 2033 on the same path) do not change.** Both are
    asserted identical path by path.
- **Paths, the same for all three dates.**
  - House: plan.draws exactly (200,000, seed 2033). VT's path inside 2031 is a Brownian bridge on each path's own
    Jan 2031 to Jan 2033 draw (its quarterly spread checks at 8.95% against 8.94%), and yields move on by 0.45 points a
    quarter. The extra draws use seed 2031.
  - History: Shiller windows since 1871, the move 36, 39 or 42 months after VT is bought; rates at today's forwards.
  - Joint replay: rates and US stocks from the same months, the move at window month 48, 51 or 54. Funds pay as modelled
    at the purchase yields (range_rule.py section 5), so its flexibility differs a little from 8i's realised figures.
- **Check:** with the move on 1 January every array equals plan.run and completion.rule (asserted): $97,605 and
  $117,142, the excess $7,503, the half kept $21,179; the house top $95,000 / $115,000 / $140,000 and 57.2% (59.8%) at least
  $115,000; history $110,000 / $115,000 / $125,000 (high was $130,000); the joint replay's top and bottom as section 4.

**Central path** (today's forwards, VT 7.5% a year; re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening, the Mon 16 Nov figure in brackets):

| | 1 Jan 2031 (register) | 1 Apr 2031 | 1 Jul 2031 |
|---|---|---|---|
| VT on the move day / the moved half | $36,698 / $18,349 ($36,989 / $18,495) | $37,362 / $18,681 ($37,659 / $18,829) | $38,038 / $19,019 ($38,340 / $19,170) |
| What the moved half pays in December 2032; the rule's cut on it | $20,138 ($20,298); 2.98% | $20,270 ($20,431); 2.84% | $20,400 ($20,562); 2.84% |
| Top before rounding / **stated top** | $117,142 ($118,072) / **$115,000** | $117,300 ($118,231) / **$115,000** | $117,426 ($118,358) / **$115,000** |
| Stated bottom | $95,000 ($97,605; was $98,380) | the same | the same |
| What the funds pay; excess | $122,503; $7,503 ($123,476; $8,476) | $122,636; $7,636 ($123,609; $8,609) | $122,766; $7,766 ($123,740; $8,740) |
| Half kept (her flexibility); her flexibility plus anything above the top (half kept plus excess; label corrected Thu 19 Nov) | $21,179; $28,682 ($21,347; $29,823) | $21,179; $28,815 ($21,347; $29,956) | $21,179; $28,945 ($21,347; $30,088) |

The cut is smaller after January because the IBTM holdings' 4 January coupon (in the engine) is already paid, so one
fewer distribution waits; April and July hold the same flows.

**House model:**

| | 1 Jan 2031 | 1 Apr 2031 | 1 Jul 2031 |
|---|---|---|---|

| Top before rounding: mean | $118,475 ($119,355) | $118,711 ($119,594) | $118,926 ($119,812) |

| Each step: $100,000 / $105,000 / $110,000 / $115,000 / $120,000 / $125,000 / $130,000 | 9.0 / 11.7 / 13.2 / 13.7 / 12.7 / 10.5 / 7.7% (was 8.3 / 11.2 / 12.9 / 13.8 / 13.0 / 11.0 / 8.2) | 8.9 / 11.6 / 13.1 / 13.6 / 12.7 / 10.5 / 7.8% | 8.8 / 11.5 / 13.0 / 13.5 / 12.6 / 10.5 / 7.8% |
| Stated bottom | $80,000 / $95,000 / $115,000 (low was $85,000) | the same, path by path | the same, path by path |

**History and the joint replay**, against 1 January, path by path:

|---|---|---|
| Stated top, 1 Jan / 1 Apr / 1 Jul: 1-in-20 low / median / 1-in-20 high | $110,000 / $115,000 / $125,000 (Jan; high was $130,000); $110,000 / $115,000 / $130,000 (Apr, Jul) | $95,000 / $115,000 / $140,000 on all three dates (Jul median was $120,000) |
| Stated top at least $115,000: Jan / Apr / Jul | 74.2% / 74.6% / 75.7% (was 80.5 / 80.6 / 80.6) | 60.7% / 61.9% / 64.3% (was 63.6 / 65.1 / 66.7) |
| Top before rounding, mean: Jan / Apr / Jul | $118,864 ($119,808) / $119,194 ($120,140) / $119,499 ($120,447) | $119,569 ($120,498) / $119,880 ($120,812) / $120,158 ($121,094) |
| 1 April: stated top lower / higher; a fall between the dates lowers it | 9.4% / 14.9%; 9.2% (was 8.9 / 16.6; 8.7) | 8.2% / 16.1%; 8.2% (was 8.0 / 15.2; 7.7) |
| 1 July: stated top lower / higher; a fall between the dates lowers it | 12.0% / 26.2%; 11.8% (was 13.2 / 25.5; 12.9) | 12.7% / 24.8%; 12.5% (was 10.8 / 27.0; 10.4) |
| Half kept: 1-in-20 low / median (all three dates) | $13,134 / $23,296 ($13,238 / $23,481) | $13,779 / $23,286 ($13,874 / $23,601) |

- **A named case:** VT bought Jan 2017, so the move falls in 2020. Moved in January the stated top is $120,000; moved in
  April, after the COVID fall (US stocks' April average 15.3% below January's), $115,000; moved in July, $120,000 again.

What it shows:
- **The date moves only the top, and only by luck.** The bottom and the half kept are the same on every path. The
  central stated top is $115,000 on all three dates (the unrounded top gains $157 by April and $284 by July (was $159 and $286): VT's
  expected return above the Treasury yield, on half of VT, for 3 or 6 months). The house median is $115,000 on all three.
- **What a later date adds is risk to the one figure co-sponsors hear.** Against 1 January the stated top moves a step
  in 28.3% of house paths by April and 38.7% by July, down in 12.0% and 15.7% (was 28.6%, 39.0%; 12.1%, 15.9%). A fall between the dates lowers it in
  11.8% and 15.4% of paths (history 9.2% and 11.8%; joint replay 8.2% and 12.5%; were 12.0%, 15.5%; 8.7%, 12.9%; 7.7%, 10.4%). Where VT falls more than 10% between
  the dates, the top steps down in more than half of the paths.
- **Her flexibility barely moves.** When the top steps down $5,000, what the funds pay falls by much less, so the
  excess the cap sends to her rises; when it steps up, the excess falls. Path by path her flexibility's median change is
  about -$20 (-$23 by April, -$20 by July; was -$21), and its 1-in-20 low moves by $12 to $37 (was about $10).

**Finding for the lead (not a decision): the evidence favours 1 January, the register's date.**
1. **The case and the IPS.** The case puts every flow "at the beginning of the applicable year" and has Laura "begin
   approaching potential co-sponsors in 2031, two years before the residency is established". So the first meeting can
   come at the start of 2031. IPS l.15 moves the half "Before you meet co-sponsors in 2031, ... so whatever figure you
   choose is money you already own". Only a move at the start of January is before every possible first meeting.
2. **A later date gains almost nothing.** It does not change the central stated top, the house median, the bottom or
   the half kept. The mean unrounded top rises by $236 (April) or $451 (July) (was $239 and $457).
3. **A later date adds a 3- or 6-month bet on shares to the top.** About 1 house path in 8 (April) or 1 in 6 (July)
   states a lower top than 1 January would have.
4. **If a meeting came before a later move,** the top would be stated before it is owned. Section 4 already prices that:
   for a top stated 3 or 6 months before the move, the move would buy less than the stated top in 6.2% or 8.6% (8.7%) of house paths (section 4's wording since Tue 17 Nov: never "not yet owned in" a share of paths).
- **Limits:** the bridge fills VT's path inside 2031 on the house model's own assumptions (normal log returns, 17.88% a
  year); yields move apart from shares; history uses monthly averages; the first trading day of each month stands for
  the 1st.

### 8s. The first plan, re-run: how a share-led plan missed its payments (Mon 16 Nov, numbers owner, MODEL; `numbers/first_plan.py`, `numbers/results_first_plan_2026-11-17.json`; re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening: 0.5% margin on the payments, 365.25-day fix: only Caplet's-plan figures in part B move, the Mon 16 Nov figure in brackets; the 27 Sep model, the history replay and the first plan on today's engine are unchanged)
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/first_plan.py` from the repo root (about 10 seconds).
  Imports the 27 Sep model by its path, and engine, plan and completion; changes no other script, writes no bytecode
  beside them, reads no results file and writes no other file.
- **The question.** The Team Reflection (3f; RANKING_4 moment 2, RANKING_5's "too bold, then too timid") tells how the
  team's first plan (its notes of Sun 27 Sep, `keel/library/council_2026-09-27/team_notes_2026-09-27.md`: a growth
  clock to 2031 at about 75% shares, a glide to 20-25% by the eve of 2033, then a Treasury "Payment Ladder" bought from
  2033) was tested that day, before any trade, and missed at least one of the ten payments in 3.2% of simulated markets,
  13.7% with a $75,000 2028 deposit and 40.8% with none. The three figures were in CLAUDE.md and RANKING_4, not here.
  Three cold readers (Mon 16 Nov) asked (a) what 3.2% meant beside the step further IPS l.11 took (reworded Tue 17 Nov evening, the lead's call on coherence reader 8, H1: "a high degree of certainty" keeps IPS l.11 as its only definition, CANON A4; the first plan was dropped for IPS l.9's reason and this check priced l.11's step; never "why 3.2% fails 'high'") and (b) how the risk works.
- **The model: the 27 Sep model, not this register's engine.** `keel/library/verified_2026-09-27/strategy_mc.py`,
  Strategy G ("growth first, buy the reserve in 2033"). Its inputs, exactly as the script states them:

  | Input | Value in the 27 Sep model |
  |---|---|
  | Paths, seed | 200,000; 20260927 |
  | Shares | J.P. Morgan 2026 LTCMA, U.S. large cap: 6.70% compound, 7.94% arithmetic, 16.47% volatility; annual returns lognormal, log mean 0.0649 and log s.d. 0.1520, solved so compound and arithmetic both match |
  | The rest | J.P. Morgan 2026 LTCMA, U.S. intermediate Treasuries: 4.00% compound, 4.06% arithmetic (3.48% volatility; log s.d. 0.0340); correlation with shares -0.01 |
  | Share weight, 2027 to 2032 | 75%, 75%, 75%, 75%, 60%, 40%, rebalanced each year |
  | Money in | $300,000 on 1 Jan 2027; the 2028 deposit ($150,000, $75,000 or none) on 1 Jan 2028, before that year's return |
  | The ten, bought 1 Jan 2033 | Ten $50,000 payments, the first that day, at one flat yield of 5.26% plus a normal draw of s.d. 1.0 point, floored at zero ("ASSUMPTION: flat curve in 2033"): $401,312 at 5.26%, $16,089 more per point lower |
  | Curve | The official par curve of Fri 25 Sep 2026: the lock-early ladder at $292,264 and the 2-year 4.81%. The growth-first design uses neither; its 5.26% is the flat rate the script takes as that curve's equivalent (it gives about $295,000 in 1 Jan 2027 money) |
  | Fees, taxes | None |
  | A miss | The money on 1 Jan 2033 is less than the ten cost that day (the script's "P(payments not fully funded)") |

- **Check (asserted).** The model is imported unchanged: 6,453 of 200,000 paths short, **3.2265%**; with a $75,000
  deposit 27,386, **13.693%**; with none 81,615, **40.8075%**. Its printed surplus percentiles ($20,000 / $131,000 /
  $226,000 / $338,000 / $540,000, as CLAUDE.md records) and its two sensitivity rows (shares 5.0% compound: 5.5%; 2033
  yield s.d. 1.5 points: 3.5%) also reproduce. The draws are rebuilt in the script's order and equal `simulate("G")`'s
  surplus path by path, so each miss can be taken apart.
- **What decides a miss: one date, 1 Jan 2033.** The growth-first design sells no share to pay a payment. Its share
  weight glides to 40% in 2032 and on 1 Jan 2033 all the money buys the ten in Treasuries. Every miss is settled that
  day, before the first payment, by the share and bond returns of 2027 to 2032 and the 2033 yield. The model has no share
  return after 2032, so it cannot test "shares sold to pay each date" or a fall in 2033-2035; the analysis below splits
  2027-2030 (the four years at 75%) from 2031-2032 instead. A shortfall that day cannot be made up later: no money comes
  in after January 2028.

**The misses, 27 Sep model** (shortfalls in 1 Jan 2033 money):

| 2028 deposit | $150,000 (the case) | $75,000 | None |
|---|---|---|---|
| Paths short (of 200,000) | **6,453 (3.23%; 1 in 31.0)** | 27,386 (13.69%; 1 in 7.3) | 81,615 (40.81%; 1 in 2.5) |
| Shortfall where short: median / 1-in-20 largest / largest | $27,695 / $92,543 / $199,697 | $37,264 / $116,026 / $242,009 | $57,549 / $151,063 / $285,401 |
| Median shortfall, share of the ten's cost that day | 6.8% | 9.2% | 14.3% |
| Shortfall less than $50,000 (1 Jan 2033 money) | 74.6% of misses | 62.4% | 44.0% |
| Payments bought in full where short, date order: median / 1-in-20 low | 9 / 7 | 8 / 6 | 8 / 5 |

**Which payment goes unpaid first,** if the money on 1 Jan 2033 buys the ten nearest first (the team's notes lock the
near payments first). Number of failing paths:

| First payment not bought in full | 2033-2036 | 2037 | 2038 | 2039 | 2040 | 2041 | 2042 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| $150,000 deposit (6,453 misses) | 0 | 1 | 22 | 177 (2.7%) | 726 (11.3%) | 1,881 (29.1%) | **3,646 (56.5%)** |
| $75,000 (27,386) | 1 (2036) | 41 | 413 | 1,808 | 4,584 | 8,382 | 12,157 |
| None (81,615) | 76 (2035-2036) | 1,125 | 4,920 | 11,491 | 18,308 | 22,073 | 23,622 |

Bought longest first instead (the IPS's build order for 2027), the same shortfall would fall on 2033. Which date carries
it is a choice made in January 2033; its size in 1 Jan 2033 money is not. In the late payment's own dollars it is larger,
since money set aside for 2042 would have earned interest to then: the median miss leaves 2042 $40,412 short, or 2033
$27,695 short.

**What drives the 3.2%** ($150,000 deposit):

| | Failing paths (6,453) | All paths |
|---|---|---|
| Shares lower after 2027-2030 than at the start | **97.5%** | 19.7% |
| Chance of a miss if shares are lower after 2027-2030 / higher | **16.0% (about 1 in 6)** / 0.10% (about 1 in 1,000) | |
| Shares over 2027-2030: 1-in-20 low / median / 1-in-20 high | -46.4% / -27.6% / -4.6% | -21.4% / +29.7% / +114.1% |
| Shares over 2027-2032: 1-in-20 low / median / 1-in-20 high | -51.1% / -32.3% / -15.7% | |
| A year with shares down 20% or more: in 2027-2030 / only in 2031-2032 / none | 53.2% / 11.3% / **35.6%** | any year: 16.3% (2.9% a year) |
| Worst single share year: 1-in-20 / median / 1-in-20 | -33.2% / -22.3% / -13.6% | median -11.5% |
| Year of the worst share return: 2027 / 2028 / 2029 / 2030 / 2031 / 2032 | 11.4 / 21.7 / 21.6 / 21.9 / 14.1 / 9.4% | 16.7% each |
| 2033 yield below the central 5.26% | 64.7% | 50% |
| Short even at the central 5.26% (same share paths) | 82.4% | 3.01% of all paths |

- **The gap, split exactly (log terms).** The model's central path (every return at its median, 6.70% and 4.00%; the
  2033 yield 5.26%) has $619,165 on 1 Jan 2033 against ten costing $401,312, 1.54 times. In the failing paths the money
  is on average 0.53 lower in logs against the cost (about 41% below the central ratio). Of that gap: 2027 9.5%, 2028
  22.2%, 2029 22.2%, 2030 22.5%, 2031 13.7%, 2032 6.9%, the 2033 yield 2.9%. **The four years at 75% shares carry
  76.5%**, the two glide years 20.5%. 2027 weighs less because its return falls on the $300,000 alone, before the 2028
  deposit; 2028 to 2030 each fall on all the money at 75%.
- **The median miss, in words** (path 161,327): shares lose 21.3% in 2027 and 5.9% in 2028, gain 11.1% in 2029, lose
  3.9% in 2030 (down 20.9% over the four years at 75%), lose 13.2% in 2031 and gain 3.0% in 2032 (down 29.3% over six
  years). The money is $389,241 on 1 Jan 2033. The 2033 yield is 4.29%, so the ten cost $416,937: $27,695 short (6.6%).
  Bought nearest first, 2033 to 2041 are paid in full and 2042 gets $9,588 of its $50,000 ($40,412 short).
- **A miss with no crash year** (path 176,353, the median of the 2,297 misses with no year down 20% or more): shares
  lose 5.5%, 17.1%, 0.3% and 4.9% in 2027-2030 (down 25.7%), then gain 7.2% and 7.5%. The 2033 yield is 3.70%, so the
  ten cost $426,854 against $406,956: $19,898 short; 2042 gets $22,399. Only 27 misses (0.4%) have no year below -10%.

**The same design on US stock history** (Keel's replay, not run on 27 Sep): US stocks with dividends (the register's
Shiller file), every 72-month window since 1871 (1,758, first purchases Jan 1871 to Jun 2017), rates fixed:

| Rates | $150,000 | $75,000 | None |
|---|---|---|---|
| The 27 Sep model's central: the rest earns 4.00% a year, the ten at 5.26% flat ($401,312) | 25 windows (1.4%); short median $50,959, largest $97,652 | 84 (4.8%) | 546 (31.1%) |
| The register's forwards: the rest in 1-year Treasuries at the 6 Nov forwards, the ten at $399,089 (8h), VT's 0.06% fee | 25 (1.4%); median $43,585, largest $90,802 | 75 (4.3%) | 487 (27.7%) |

- **All 25 failing windows start from Oct 1927 to Oct 1929:** one episode, the 1929 crash. Started Jan 1929 (shares
  -9.5%, -22.7%, -44.2% and -5.8% in the first four years): $81,508 short. Started Jan 1937: $50,602 over; Jan 1973:
  $128,639 over; Jan 2000: $71,199 over; Oct 2007: $206,521 over (27 Sep rates).

**The same design on today's engine** (Keel's re-run, MODEL). The register's house model: the same 200,000 paths as
section 4 (plan.draws, seed 2033; VT's 2028-2030 and 2031-2032 growth split into years by Brownian bridges; VT's 2027
growth and the yield moves of 2031-2032 drawn with seed 2027), VT at 7.5% and 17.88% less its 0.06% fee, yields
parallel at 0.9 points a year and apart from shares, the 6 Nov curve. The rest is in 1-year Treasuries rolled each
January (variant: a Treasury fund of 6.95 years' duration, IEF-like). On 1 Jan 2033 the ten are bought at that day's
curve (the 6 Nov curve moved by the path's yield change, par yields floored at zero; section 1's payment-date basis):
$399,089 unmoved, 8h's figure. Central path: $645,366 against $399,089, 1.62 times.

| 2028 deposit | $150,000 | $75,000 | None |
|---|---|---|---|
| The rest in 1-year Treasuries: paths short | **9,953 (4.98%; 1 in 20)** | 29,506 (14.75%) | 72,916 (36.46%) |
| The same: shortfall median / 1-in-20 largest / largest | $37,737 / $130,846 / $251,826 | $48,163 / $151,452 / $294,818 | $66,713 / $183,877 / $340,502 |
| The rest in a 6.95-year Treasury fund: paths short | 8,147 (4.07%; 1 in 25) | 26,801 (13.40%) | 70,678 (35.34%) |
| Caplet's plan on the same paths: escrow standard met (section 2) | 100% | 99.965% (70 paths fail, all after yields fell 0.89 to 1.57 points before January 2027; was 99.97%, 55; section 5) | 2 paths (0.001%) |

- With the rest in 1-year Treasuries: 91.5% of misses had VT lower after 2027-2030 (a miss in 21.7% of those paths);
  81.3% had yields lower by 2033 than today's forwards; with the 2033 curve unmoved the miss rate is 3.64%.
- **Why higher than 3.2%:** VT's spread (a log s.d. of 17.88% against the 27 Sep model's 15.2%) and yields that keep
  moving to 2033 (s.d. 2.2 points by then, against 1.0 point), which reprice the ten; faster growth (7.5% against 6.7%)
  offsets part.
- **Caplet's plan in the 9,953 paths where the first plan misses:** all ten paid under the escrow standard in every one;
  stated range $90,000 to $105,000 at the median (bottom $80,000 / $90,000 / $110,000, top $90,000 / $105,000 /
  $125,000 at the 1-in-20 low / median / 1-in-20 high); half kept median $9,939 ($10,067). The markets that break the first plan
  give Caplet's plan a smaller range, not a missed payment.
- **With no 2028 deposit,** Caplet's ladder alone costs more than $300,000 in January 2027 in 58.6% of house paths (median
  $8,114 unbought where it does; centrally $2,287, so the 2033 payment is $3,016 short at forward rates and the ten
  $57,455 short at 0%, section 5).

What it shows:
- **About 1 in 31.** The 27 Sep 3.2% is 6,453 of 200,000 simulated markets, 1 in 31.0. Under the standard Caplet's plan
  meets (section 2: all ten paid if no coupon earns anything), the count is none: by construction in every house path
  (the deposit covers the completion in all 200,000) and in all 585 historical rate windows. With a $75,000 deposit
  the first plan misses in about 1 in 7 (13.7%); Caplet's plan still meets the standard in 99.965% of house paths (70 of 200,000; was 99.97%, 55).
- **What "none" cost Laura (Tue 17 Nov evening, for aha row 10's fix after cold test 4; Keel arithmetic, MODEL, not yet in `first_plan.py`).** Measured as the money the same two deposits leave above the ten on 1 Jan 2033 (1 Jan 2033 money), path by path on the same draws:
  - **In the 27 Sep model's own terms** (`strategy_mc.py` imported unchanged, its Treasury-first Strategy L against the first plan, G; seed 20260927): median $207,290 against $226,096, so **about $19,000 less at the median (8%)**, $35,305 less on average; the first plan leaves more in 56.0% of markets. At the 1-in-20 low the Treasury-first plan leaves $159,224, the first plan $19,990. With a $75,000 deposit: $109,030 against $125,385 at the median ($16,355 less).
  - **On today's engine, Caplet's own plan** (the funds' payout plus the half kept, with the backstop at its forward value, $46,566, against the first plan with the rest in 1-year Treasuries; plan.draws, seed 2033 and seed 2027 as above): median $191,066 against $256,543, about **$65,000 less at the median**; centrally $190,248 ($115,000 + $28,682 + $46,566) against $246,277, $56,029 less. The first plan leaves more in 65.0% of paths; at the 1-in-20 low it leaves $280, Caplet's plan $158,942.
  - So the 27 Sep check priced "1 in 31 against none" at about $19,000 of the median money above the ten; Caplet's plan, which keeps more out of shares than the 27 Sep Treasury plan did, gives up about $65,000 at the median on today's engine for a 1-in-20 low of about $159,000 instead of almost nothing. Limits: the backstop is held at its forward value in every path; the 27 Sep Strategy L is not Caplet's design (60% shares in a sleeve, a 2031 floor in a 2-year Treasury); neither comparison includes taxes or the funds' costs beyond their fees.
- **The 27 Sep model's "never" for the Treasury plan is an assumption, not a result.** Strategy L's 0.0% is written into
  the code ("ladder covers all ten payments by construction"); on its 25 Sep curve the ten cost $292,264, under
  $300,000. At forward rates alone the ladder falls short in many history windows (section 2, design A: more often than
  not for start dates since 1981, far less often before, coherence reader 8, L9); the escrow standard is what makes "none" true. So 3f's "none" rests
  on section 2, not on the 27 Sep run.
- **"None" needs the 2028 deposit's first dollars.** It holds with $150,000 and with $75,000, not with no deposit. So
  3.2% and 13.7% can be set against none; 40.8% cannot (on its own standard Caplet's plan is then short too: the
  ten $57,455 short at 0%, section 5; never quote the $3,016 at forward rates alone). A missing deposit is one of the IPS's two reasons to change course.
- **How the risk works (the readers' point b).** The first plan bought the ten on 1 Jan 2033 with whatever its shares had
  become, so a miss is settled that day, before any payment. 97.5% of misses had shares lower after the four years at
  three-quarters; when shares were lower a miss came about 1 time in 6, when higher about 1 in 1,000. Those four years
  carry three-quarters of the gap; the 2033 yield about 3%. It needs no crash: in 35.6% of misses no year fell 20%, only
  several poor ones. And a gap found that January cannot be closed, because no money comes after January 2028.
- **Not "early payment years draining later ones", and not "one of ten dates".** The model has no share return after
  2032 and sells no share to pay a payment. So "shares sold to pay each date" is not what 3.2% measured, and card Q47 row
  3's and SKELETON 3f's "out of place on one of ten dates" / "registration on ten dates" read, on this model, as one date:
  1 Jan 2033, the day the ten had to be bought.
- **What a miss looks like.** Median $27,695 in 1 Jan 2033 money, 6.8% of the ten's cost; under $50,000 in 3 misses of
  4; 1 miss in 20 is $92,543 or more. Bought nearest first, 2033 to 2036 are always paid and the last payments carry
  it: 2042 first in 56.5% of misses, 2041 in 29.1%. Bought longest first, 2033 would carry it.
- **History is kinder; today's engine harsher.** The same design misses in 25 of 1,758 US stock windows since 1871 (1.4%),
  all of them purchases from Oct 1927 to Oct 1929; bought in Jan 2000 or Oct 2007 it would have paid the ten. On today's
  engine it misses in about 1 path in 20 (4.98%; 4.07% with a 6.95-year bond fund), mainly because yields that fall by
  2033 raise the price of the ten. The reflection quotes the 27 Sep figures, dated ("in our simulations" on 27 Sep);
  history and today's engine are context, never a replacement.
- **Limits:** the 27 Sep model draws independent lognormal years for US large caps and an index of intermediate
  Treasuries, with no fees or taxes and one flat 2033 yield drawn apart from everything else; it prices the ten as ten
  discounted payments at that yield (no reinvestment risk after 2033, not the escrow standard), which favours the first
  plan, and its miss is a yes or no on one date. History: monthly averages, US stocks for world shares, rates fixed, about
  25 independent six-year spans, one failing episode. Today's engine: the house model's yield walk is carried past 2031 to
  2033 (the register stops it at 2031, section 7); shares and yields independent; the rest's two holdings are Keel's
  choice; the ten on the payment-date basis, not the escrow standard, which would cost the first plan more. Not run: a
  variant in which the first plan kept shares past 2033 and sold them to pay each date (the readers' reading). It was not
  the team's plan.

### 8t. Custody: the largest balance at one broker (Mon 16 Nov night, numbers owner, MODEL; `numbers/custody.py`, `numbers/results_custody_2026-11-17.json`; re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening: 0.5% margin on the payments, 365.25-day fix, the Mon 16 Nov night file `results_custody_2026-11-16.json` is the record and its figures are in brackets: the margin's extra Treasuries move about $840 from VT and the late-2032 funds into the reserve, so the peak barely moves)
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/custody.py` from the repo root (about 10 seconds). Imports
  engine, plan and completion and changes none of them; reads no results file.
- **Why.** SKELETON item 4(c)'s custody line, aha row 5b and the cards behind them set "$589,623 at the end" (8k) against
  SIPC's $500,000 cover (of which at most $250,000 cash). 8k's $589,623 is all of Laura's money valued on 1 Jan 2033 for
  its 87.2% / 12.8% split: the reserve, the $115,000 contribution carried 17 days from December 2032, the excess and the
  half kept. It is a dated broker balance only if the contribution were still at the broker on the morning of 1 Jan 2033.
  This section values what the plan holds at one broker (every holding at one broker: card Q78) on every month-end
  from 31 Jan 2028 to 31 Dec 2032, and on 1 Jan 2033 before the first payment.
- **Method: the plan as registered, no new assumption.** The 6 Nov 2026 curve's forward rates, VT at its median 7.5% a
  year (plan.run's central path, filled in between 1 Jan 2028, 1 Jan 2031 and 1 Jan 2033 at a constant rate); the escrow
  standard (the ten own rungs plus the backstop, section 2); Keel's reading of "most"; the move on 1 Jan 2031. Fees as
  the forward world charges them: the iBond funds' 0.07% and VT's 0.06%; waiting money earns forward short rates (the
  Treasury-bill ETF's 0.15% belongs to the design test, section 0). Each holding at nominal market value on the date:
  - the ten rungs and the backstop as 8h values them: each coupon, distribution or fund payout already received waits
    in the Treasury-bill ETF, grown at forwards; each flow still to come is discounted on the forwards to that date. On
    1 Jan 2033 this gives 8h's **$445,655** (asserted);
  - the floor ($81,244, bought Mon 3 Jan 2028) and the moved half ($18,349, 1 Jan 2031) as plan.run grows them, to
    $102,365 and $20,138 on 15 Dec 2032 (asserted); their payout then waits in the Treasury-bill ETF;
  - VT: $29,594 in January 2028, $36,698 on 1 Jan 2031, the half kept $21,179 on 1 Jan 2033 (asserted).
  - With every part counted to 1 Jan 2033 the script gives 8k's **$589,623** (asserted), so the two figures are one
    valuation read on different days.
- **The contribution's day is not set.** 8k has it paid in December 2032; the funds' cash reaches Laura Mon 20 to Wed
  22 Dec 2032 (8h). **Reading A:** it leaves then, before 31 Dec 2032 (the excess, $7,503, stays). **Reading B:** it
  waits at the broker to 1 Jan 2033.

| | Reading A (contribution leaves about 22 Dec 2032) | Reading B (it waits to 1 Jan 2033) |
|---|---|---|
| **Largest month-end balance** | **$587,008 ($587,039) on Tue 30 Nov 2032**, $87,008 ($87,039) over $500,000 | **$589,541 ($589,573) on Fri 31 Dec 2032**, $89,541 ($89,573) over |
| Of it: Treasury bonds / iBond ETFs / Treasury-bill ETF (waiting money) | $161,391 / $178,628 ($177,790) (IBTM to IBTR) / $103,687 ($103,420) | $162,078 / $139,027 ($138,185) (IBTO to IBTR; IBTM has paid out) / $144,490 ($144,221) |
| Of it: the floor and the moved half (late-2032 fund, or its payout waiting) / VT (the half kept) | $102,158 ($102,969) + $20,097 ($20,256) (in the fund) / $21,047 ($21,214) | $122,772 ($123,746) (payout waiting) / $21,175 ($21,343) |
| Of it: uninvested cash | $0 | $0 |
| Daily, between month-ends | $588,231 ($588,262) on Wed 15 Dec 2032 (the funds pay); $588,722 ($588,754) on Tue 21 Dec 2032, the day before the contribution leaves | rises to $589,623 on 1 Jan 2033 |
| 31 Dec 2032 / 1 Jan 2033 before the first payment | $474,289 ($474,321) / $474,355 ($474,387) (after it, $424,355; was $424,387) | $589,541 / $589,623 |

- **When it passes $500,000: the end of August 2029.** $462,496 ($462,499) on 1 Jan 2028 after the purchases; $499,132 ($499,144) on Tue
  31 Jul 2029; **$501,210 ($501,224) on Fri 31 Aug 2029**; then above $500,000 at every month-end to 30 Nov 2032 (40 month-ends,
  reading A; 41 in reading B, with 31 Dec 2032). So the custody exposure is not "at the end": it runs for about three
  years and four months, from late 2029 until the contribution leaves.
- **At net asset value** (the iBond funds as a broker's statement shows them, gross holdings less the fee already
  charged, instead of 8h's value after the fee still to come): peak $587,192 ($587,222) on 30 Nov 2032; first above $500,000 still
  31 Aug 2029 ($501,731, was $501,741). The headline keeps 8h's convention, so that it agrees with $445,655 and $589,623.
- **Cash against SIPC's $250,000.** The model holds no uninvested cash at any month-end: every waiting dollar is in the
  Treasury-bill ETF, an ETF share. If each month's receipts sat as cash until the month-end, the most before December
  2032 is $3,645 ($3,639) (31 May 2028). At 31 Dec 2032, IBTM's own payout ($39,433 on 15 Dec 2032) and the month's distributions
  are $40,361; with the funds' payout waiting as cash too, $47,880 ($48,855) (A, with the excess) or $163,133 ($164,107) (B). Under $250,000
  in every case.
- **How firm "over $500,000" is on 30 Nov 2032.** Yields that day 1 point lower: $600,005 ($600,010); 1 point higher: $574,732 ($574,789);
  2 points higher: $563,130 ($563,212). It takes a parallel rise of 8.7 points that day to bring it under $500,000: by then the
  floor and the moved half are cash inside their fund (its holdings matured about July 2032), VT is $21,047, and the
  reserve's bonds and iBond ETFs ($340,019, was $339,181) have a duration of 3.7 years.

What it shows:
- **The figure for the custody line:** at one broker the plan holds more than SIPC's $500,000 from the end of August
  2029 until the contribution leaves in December 2032, at most about **$587,000** at a month-end (30 Nov 2032, today's
  forwards), **about $87,000 over**. $589,623 is the same money one month later, and only if the contribution had not
  yet left; never call it "the balance at the end".
- **After the contribution leaves the balance is under $500,000:** $474,355 on 1 Jan 2033 before the first payment
  (8k's "about $474,000" is this figure), $424,355 ($424,387) after it, and lower each year.
- **What sits above $500,000 is mostly Treasuries and Treasury funds.** At the peak, $102,158 + $20,097 of facility money
  in the late-2032 fund and $21,047 of VT; the rest is the reserve. SIPC's $500,000 caps only its own advance above her
  share of the broker's customer property, so the $87,008 ($87,039) is at risk only if that pool is short by more than SIPC fills
  (card Q72 row 13); the history is delay, not loss, where assets were kept apart (cards Q72, Q78).
- **Limits:** the central path only (forward rates; VT on a smooth 7.5% path); the house model's yield walk stops in 2031
  (section 7), so no distribution is given. Markets before the peak move it: in the house model the floor's payout runs
  $87,920 to $123,582 ($88,047 to $124,380), the moved half's $10,276 to $34,029 ($10,581 to $34,308) and the half kept $9,621 to $40,890 (1-in-20 low to high,
  section 4, each its own percentiles), tens of thousands either way on a peak $87,008 over the line; shares alone take
  out at most the moved half and the half kept ($41,144 centrally at the peak, was $41,470), which still leaves $545,864 ($545,569). It values month-ends
  and the days named only; the engine pays the iBond funds' distributions twice a year, not monthly, which moves the
  month-end cash figures a little and, at forward rates, the balances not at all; one broker is the plan's set-up (card Q78).
- **Files that set $589,623 (or "about $589,000 / $589,700") against SIPC** are listed in section 10 (Mon 16 Nov night
  block), not edited.

### 8u. What it takes to break each promise (Wed 18 Nov morning, numbers owner, MODEL; card Q109; `numbers/break_test.py`, `numbers/results_break_2026-11-18.json`)
- **Why.** 3e's assumptions and limits is the report's lowest cell (SCORECARD: clarity 2; the evening lay reader of Tue
  17 Nov: "about 200 words still carry some twenty ideas"). Card Q109 gave the shape of a reverse stress test: "define
  what constitutes business failure and then detail what events could drive that outcome" (PRA 2025 draft, 8.2). This
  section is that table for Laura's promises: for each, the failure in plain words, the events that would drive it and
  the size each event would need. IPS l.17's two reasons to change the plan are a different list (below, "The two
  lists"; card Q109 row 33).
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/break_test.py` from the repo root (about 30 seconds).
  Imports engine, plan and completion and changes none of them; reads no results file; writes only
  `results_break_2026-11-18.json`. It re-derives every register figure the table uses that the engine can give and
  asserts it (the first $39,162 and the $2,388 top-up; 3.57 and 3.89 points; $40,821 / $42,569 / $46,351 for falls during
  2027; the missed deposit's $3,016 and $57,455; 0.50% and 0.74% covered; $74,031 at forwards at 0.5%; the central
  covers 2.32%, 6.12% and 3.16%; the floor's $102,365; the unrounded bottom $97,605; Table 3 row 6's $85,000; 8p's +10.6%
  and -21.2%; her flexibility plus anything above the top, $28,682 (label corrected Thu 19 Nov), and 8q's 24.9%; the deletion test's $7,365). Every assert passes.
- **What it computes that the register did not have (new, MODEL):** (1) the payments' shortfall, and the interest cash
  waiting would need, if fund costs beyond the 0.07% fee pass the 0.5% margin (0.75%, 1%, 2% of every iBond fund flow;
  the same ledger and cut as `cost_cover.py` part B); (2) the stated bottom's own cover, the floor alone, at its
  expected payout centrally and in every house path; (3) the fall (or rise) in yields that moves the bottom she will be
  able to state by one $5,000 step, before 1 Jan 2027 (exact re-sizing, as section 5(e)) and during 2027 alone (plan.run
  with `completion.margin_fn`, as section 5's "A fall during 2027"); (4) the missed deposit with the short-term Treasury
  ETF's fee. Keel arithmetic is marked.
- **Basis.** The register as re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening (design H_plain with the fee cushion and the payments' 0.5%
  margin; the range rule on the fund's holdings; Keel's reading of "most"), the 6 Nov 2026 curve (section 0), central
  path at today's forwards with VT at 7.5%, house model 200,000 paths, seed 2033. Later curves are disclosed, never
  re-booked (below).
- **Three verbs, kept apart.** A promise *breaks* when a figure Laura owes or has stated is not paid on its date. A
  figure not yet stated is *lowered* (it breaks nothing: nothing is stated before it is owned, IPS l.15). Her
  flexibility *shrinks* or is *drawn on* (it is promised to no one, IPS l.15, so it breaks no promise).

| Promise (owned from) | It breaks if | What would have to happen (each event and the size it needs) | What only lowers or shrinks it | One figure for 3e |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **The ten payments** (most of each owned from January 2027, all ten from January 2028; the standard: paid even if cash waiting earns nothing, with the 0.5% margin for fund costs beyond the fee, section 2) | A payment is short of $50,000, or late, on its 1 January | (a) **A US default**: any Treasury payment more than five business days late (Table 3 row 2); one event, any size. (b) **No 2028 deposit by 31 March 2028**: the ten $57,455 short if cash waiting earns nothing ($58,775 with the short-term Treasury ETF's fee, new), the 2033 payment $3,016 short at today's market rates, short in all 585 historical windows (section 5). (c) **A deposit smaller than the first slice the payments need**: under $39,162 at today's rates, a cut of more than 73.9%; a $30,000 / $20,000 / $10,000 deposit leaves the ten $11,647 / $27,201 / $42,756 short at zero interest; at today's market rates they are paid while the deposit covers the $2,388 top-up (98.4%) (section 5). (d) **A fall in yields before the ten are bought (Mon 4 Jan 2027) that makes the first slice larger than the whole deposit**: 3.57 points (3.89 for the ten at today's market rates; sections 1, 5). With the deposit in full no fall during 2027 can: at zero rates the first slice is $60,393 (section 5, "When most cannot hold" (b)), so the standard survives a deposit 59.7% smaller whatever 2027 brings (Keel arithmetic). (e) **Fund costs beyond the 0.07% fee larger than the 0.5% margin, with cash waiting earning too little**: covered to 0.50% of every fund flow under the standard, 0.74% without the ETF's fee (section 2); at 0.75% / 1% / 2%, **$593 / $1,187 / $3,561 short** (on 2036 and 2037) unless waiting money earns at least **0.16% / 0.32% / 0.95% a year** before the ETF's 0.15% (new). (f) **Late, and short only if the broker had lost customers' securities beyond what SIPC fills**: a broker failure while the account holds more than SIPC's $500,000 (end of August 2029 to December 2032; at most $87,008 over, 30 Nov 2032; the history is delay, not loss, where assets were kept apart; 8t) (Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner: was "Late, not short", which the plan does not guarantee; bond desk 5, M7, as SKELETON 3e note 1(d) since Wed 18 Nov evening) | Nothing breaks them: any fall in share prices (VT worthless, all ten paid: section 4's deletion test); any move in yields once the first slice is bought in January 2028; "most" giving way (section 5) | **About three-quarters**: at today's rates the 2028 deposit could arrive 73.9% smaller and all ten would still be paid even if cash waiting earns nothing (AHA_REGISTER "The order of purchase", fixed wording: "only a deposit more than about three-quarters smaller leaves the payments short of that standard") |
| **The stated bottom** ($95,000 centrally; owned from January 2028, when the floor is bought; stated in 2031) | The floor pays less than the bottom she stated, or its money reaches the contribution late: a stated figure goes unpaid or late (her credibility with co-sponsors), never "a smaller contribution than hoped" (card Q109 rows 4, 42) (Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner: "or late" added, coherence reader 9, M7; "breaks" already meant "not paid on its date", above) | (a) **A US default** (the same event as the payments'). (b) **Fund costs beyond the two fees larger than the cover under the bottom**: centrally 3.16% of the floor's value at zero interest (section 3), **7.20% of its expected payout** (new); at least 0.50% in every house path by construction (**1-in-20 low 0.76%, median 3.03%**, new; at the expected payout, least 1.90%, median 7.15%, new); costs of 0.75% / 1% would break it, at zero interest, in 4.8% / 9.8% of house paths (9,691 / 19,688; new in `break_test.py`, the floor alone; section 3's 6 / 9 paths are on another basis, label corrected Wed 18 Nov evening). (c) **One step not taken**: the floor's $15,446 of payouts before its end kept in the short-term Treasury ETF until the contribution is paid (section 3). (d) **Late** (added Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner; coherence reader 9, M7): a broker failure while the account holds more than SIPC's $500,000, row 1's (f), the same event, not sized again. The floor and its payout sit at the broker until the contribution leaves ($102,158 in the late-2032 fund at the 30 Nov 2032 peak, 8t), so a failure then delays the contribution as well as the 2033 payment; short only if the broker had lost customers' securities beyond what SIPC fills (bond desk 5, M7). Not in its set: a missed deposit (no bottom is stated: "no range until paid", Table 3 row 1; card Q109 row 31); any share price (VT worthless: still $95,000) | Before January 2028 it is not yet owned, so these lower the bottom she will be able to state and break nothing: **a fall in yields of about 0.10 points before 1 Jan 2027 takes it to $90,000, 0.28 points to $85,000, 1.31 points to $80,000; during 2027 alone 0.38, 1.12 and 1.87 points; a rise of 0.09 points before 1 Jan 2027 takes it to $100,000** (new; section 4's "Rounding is fragile at the centre", seen from the yield side); 1 point before 1 Jan 2027 gives $85,000 (Table 3 row 6, reproduced); a $112,500 deposit gives $65,000 (section 5) | About 3% (3.16%), 11.7 times the frictions measured (about 0.27%, section 0; Keel arithmetic). **The two lists (card Q109 rows 25, 31-33; IPS l.17; CANON A34).**
- IPS l.17's two reasons to change the plan: a missed deposit and a Treasury default. They are exits for when the plan
  cannot be carried out, never for when keeping it would hurt (Q7b-033).
- The ways a promise breaks, from the table: **a US default is in all three promises' sets** (the common cause, card
  Q109 row 25: named once in 3e, nowhere else attached, Book 19); **a missed deposit is in the payments' set only**: it
  can never make a stated figure untrue, because none is stated before it is owned; **fund costs beyond the margin (the payments) or the room (a stated figure) are
  in all three sets and are not a reason to change** (label corrected Thu 19 Nov, coherence reader 10, M3: was "beyond the margin" for all three) (Table 3 row 4 closes a fund event with an act); **a broker failure is in all three sets as lateness, and is not a reason to change** (added Fri 20 Nov morning, numbers owner; coherence reader 9, M7: rows 2-3 now carry it as their (d)); a deposit smaller
  than the first slice and a fall of 3.57 points before January 2027 are in the payments' set and are not reasons either
  (a smaller deposit runs the same order, CANON D9). **No fall in any price is in any set**: IPS l.17's "A fall in the
  market is not one of them", tested from the failure end.
- So 3e never says "only two things can break the plan" (card Q109 row 33) and never that the two reasons are the ways
  the plan can fail.

**Later curves (disclosed, never re-booked; section 0).** On the 13 Nov 2026 curve (section 5, "On a later curve") the
first slice is $32,074, so the deposit could arrive 78.6% smaller (Keel arithmetic); the first slice moves with yields
until the ten are bought on Mon 4 Jan 2027, so "about three-quarters" is said only "at today's rates", with the curve's
date in a table note. (Dated line, Wed 18 Nov evening, numbers owner: exact on that curve the first slice is $31,859,
so 78.8%; $32,074 and 78.6% were card Q98's parallel stand-in; section 4, "On a later curve (Wed 18 Nov evening)",
where the same rule states $105,000 to $125,000 on 13 Nov.)

**For card Q109's owner (the card is not edited; its figures predate the Tue 17 Nov evening re-size):** row 29's "no
margin until the re-size planned for Wed 18 Nov" (done Tue 17 Nov evening; the two-event set is now costs above 0.5% with
waiting cash under 0.32% a year at a 1% cost, this section), 3.60 (now 3.57), "1.33 points ... 0.09%" (1.30 points, 0.20%,
section 5(d)) and "about 0.06% a year" (a 0.1% cost now needs none); row 30 and verdict 2's 3.09%, 3.92% and 6.86% (2.32%,
3.16% and 6.12%) and $15,569 and $1,162 ($15,446 and $1,153); row 33's "a few hundred dollars" (now: $0 to 0.5%, then
about $240 for each 0.1% beyond, $593 at 0.75%); row 34 (Table 3 row 4's gap now holds at zero interest to 0.5%); row 36's
$474,387 ($474,355); rows 37 and claim 3's $29,823 and 25.9% ($28,682 and 24.9%); row 38's 11.4 times (8.6 for the top,
11.7 for the bottom).

**Words for 3e (each checked against WORDS, CANON and Book 19).** "About three-quarters" only with "at today's rates";
"about 2%" only "on today's figures" and "in all", of what the late-2032 fund pays on its two purchases behind the top (January 2028 and the shares moved in January 2031), never "two Treasury funds", "the two funds behind the top" or "its two Treasury funds" (one fund, IBTM, bought twice: Table 1 prints it once; bond desk 5, H1; the 2.32% is 1 less the top over that pot's zero-interest value, $117,731, `break_test.py`), and never called the margin (it is the room under the top); "about 25%" only "if shares earn the 7.5% a year assumed" (bond desk 5, H2), never "on today's figures" alone, which is note 2's zero-interest tag (bond desk 5, L6; corrected Thu 19 Nov: was "with 'on today's figures'"), and never "about a
quarter" (WORDS: one phrase, one quantity), "cushion" or "smallest when needed". "Certain" takes the stated range as its subject, never a figure. Treasuries may be
called guaranteed or risk-free, with no "barring a US default" (Book 19); the default is named once, as the IPS's own
condition. Never "fail-safe", never "only two things can break the plan", never a stated contribution above the stated
top, never a probability rarer than 1 in 100 in prose (so the new house shares in rows 2 and 3 stay here).

**The about 25% at other share returns (Thu 19 Nov morning, numbers owner; bond desk 5, H2; `numbers/flex_cover.py`,
`numbers/results_flex_cover_2026-11-19.json`). MODEL; new figures, no existing figure moves.**
- **Why.** Of the $28,682 behind row 4's "about 25%", $21,179 is the half of VT kept, grown at J.P. Morgan's 7.5% a
  year; only the $7,503 above the top comes from Treasury rates. So the figure moves with the share forecast, and 3e
  says it "if shares earn the 7.5% a year assumed" (bond desk 5, H2: "not written" until a register line carried the
  other returns). This block books the cover at 8v's other three returns.
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/flex_cover.py` from the repo root (about 10 seconds).
  Imports engine, plan and completion and changes none of them; copies 8v's central path and house draws from
  `forecast_blocks.py` (importing it would rewrite its results file); reads no results file; writes only
  `results_flex_cover_2026-11-19.json`. It asserts 8u's $28,682 and 24.9%, 8v's pots ($27,159, $24,959, $27,316) and
  half kept ($20,212, $18,828, $17,185), the stated ends (the bottom $95,000 in every row; the top $110,000 at 3.1%,
  $115,000 at the others; the contribution equal to the stated top in every row), 8q's house 15.8% (9.3%) at 7.5%,
  and bond desk 5's 23.6% and 21.7%. Every assert passes.
- **Basis.** As 8v: the register as re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening, the 6 Nov 2026 curve, the move on 1 Jan 2031; central
  path at today's forwards with VT at (1 + r) cubed to the move and squared after; house paths 200,000 (seed 2033) with
  VT's median moved to r and J.P. Morgan's 17.88% spread kept (at 7.5% the register's own draws, number for number). The
  cover is 8q's: the pot (the half kept plus anything above the top) over the contribution, which equals the stated top,
  so a rise of that share in the US-dollar cost of a building budget set on her top uses up the pot. The house figure
  is the rise covered 19 times in 20 (the 5th percentile of pot over contribution, as 8q and `taiwan_joint.py`).

| World shares a year (VT, before its 0.06% fee) | Stated top, central | Her flexibility plus anything above the top (half kept + above the top) | Rise it covers on the stated top, central (on $115,000) | House: rise covered 19 times in 20 (half kept alone); median |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1% (card Q124's lowest sum; 8v) | $110,000 | $27,316 ($17,185 + $10,130) | **24.8%** (23.8%) | 14.2% (7.6%); 21.8% |
| 5.0% (card Q124's middle; Syzygy, 8p) | $115,000 | $24,959 ($18,828 + $6,131) | **21.7%** | 14.8% (8.3%); 23.0% |
| 6.5% (the median of fifteen houses' forecasts, card Q6b) | $115,000 | $27,159 ($20,212 + $6,946) | **23.6%** | 15.4% (8.9%); 24.0% |
| **7.5% (J.P. Morgan 2027; the register)** | **$115,000** | **$28,682 ($21,179 + $7,503)** | **24.9%** (8u row 4, 8q) | **15.8% (9.3%); 24.8%** (8q) |

- **Bond desk 5's figures reproduce:** 23.6% at 6.5% (engine 23.62%) and 21.7% at 5.0% (21.70%), 8v's pots over
  $115,000 exactly as the desk took them. **New:** 3.1% on its own $110,000 top, 24.8% (24.83%; 23.75% on a $115,000
  budget); the half kept alone on the stated top centrally 15.6% / 16.4% / 17.6% / 18.4% (3.1% to 7.5%); the house rows
  at 3.1%, 5.0% and 6.5%.
- **The central order is not the forecast's.** It is lowest at 5.0%, where the top clears its step by $811 and little
  sits above it; at 3.1% the top states $5,000 lower and that $5,000 stays with her (8v), so a larger pot meets a
  smaller top. Across the house paths the order is the forecast's: covered 19 times in 20 from 15.8% (7.5%) to 14.2%
  (3.1%), the median from 24.8% to 21.8%.
- **3.1% is not "J.P. Morgan's cash".** It is 8v's row for card Q124's lowest sum (3.06%, run at 3.1%). J.P. Morgan's
  2026 edition put cash at 3.10% (CLAUDE.md's verified facts, 27 Sep); the 2027 edition's cash figure is not in this
  register, so the row is never labelled with it.
- **Notes for 3e (not wording).** "About 25%, if shares earn the 7.5% a year assumed" stays the one figure (row 4). If
  3e wants the spread: at each of the four returns tested, 3.1% to 7.5% a year, it covers about 22% to 25% centrally
  (21.7% to 24.9%; Keel arithmetic on this table). Never "about 25% whatever shares do" (at 7.5%, 1 house path in 20
  covers 15.8% or less); never "about a quarter" (WORDS); each cover is of her part of the building budget, the stated
  top ($110,000 at 3.1%), never of the whole building's budget (bond desk 5, H2).
- **Limits.** As 8v (one spread at every median; stocks and yields drawn apart in the house paths) and 8q (the need on
  the contribution, at face; the national index is not her building).

**Limits.** Central path and house paths only; fund costs taken as a flat share off every iBond fund flow; yields move
in parallel; the engine's IBTM holdings stand in for the floor fund (section 0); the bottom's one-step yields before 1
Jan 2027 use section 5's exact re-sizing and during 2027 plan.run's duration pricing of the unbought tail (within about
$40 of the exact, section 5 (b)); a step in the bottom is a $5,000 rounding edge, so its yield thresholds are small by
construction and say nothing about odds.

### 8v. The share forecast from its building blocks (Wed 18 Nov morning, numbers owner's worker, MODEL; card Q124; `numbers/forecast_blocks.py`, `numbers/results_forecast_blocks_2026-11-18.json`)
- **Why.** Card Q124 rebuilt world shares' expected return from today's dividend yield, inflation, growth, buybacks and
  valuation: a sum near 5% a year (3.1% to 6.4% across its anchors), against the register's 7.5%, J.P. Morgan's 2027
  estimate, above the middle of published forecasts (median about 6.5% of the fifteen houses; about 6.9% of seventeen with Goldman Sachs's 7.7% and Fidelity International's 7.6%, card Q146, Wed 18 Nov evening; the report gives no median figure). It read three claims off 8p without a run. This
  section runs them on the register's engine. No forecast is called wrong here: each row is a "what if", and 7.5% stays
  the register's input (section 0; CANON B15).
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/forecast_blocks.py` from the repo root (about 8 seconds).
  Imports engine, plan and completion and changes none of them; reads no results file; writes only
  `results_forecast_blocks_2026-11-18.json`. It asserts every register figure it touches: section 4's central row to the
  cent (unrounded $97,605.08 and $117,142.45; funds pay $122,503.13; $7,503.13; $21,179.18; $28,682.31; the completion,
  floor money, VT bought and VT on the move day); 8p's Syzygy and median rows ($115,811 and $18,828; $116,602 and
  $20,212); 8p's +10.6% (3.4% a year) and 35.4%; section 4's house figures at 7.5% (57.2% at $115,000 or more; top
  $95,000 / $115,000 / $140,000; bottom $80,000 / $95,000 / $115,000; 8f's $20,486); and that its house draws at 7.5%
  equal `plan.draws` number for number. Every assert passes.
- **Basis.** The register as re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening (H_plain with the fee cushion and the payments' 0.5% margin, the
  range rule on the fund's holdings, Keel's reading of "most", the 6 Nov 2026 curve, the move on 1 Jan 2031).
  - *Central:* today's forwards, VT at (1 + r) cubed to the move and squared after (8p's house panel).
  - *House paths:* the register's 200,000 (seed 2033) with VT's median moved to r and J.P. Morgan's 17.88% spread kept;
    the same random numbers, so only the median moves.
  - *Shares alone:* rates at today's forwards; the chance VT is under the +10.6% the $115,000 step needs at the move
    (lognormal arithmetic, as 8p's 35.4%).
  - *The valuation case:* card Q124 row 18's input, J.P. Morgan's valuation block of -0.65% a year, which takes ACWI's
    P/E from 20.82 to about 19.5 over ten years. The case keeps a 5.0% ten-year return but lands that whole fall in the
    three years from January 2028 to the move; the other blocks earn 5.69% a year throughout (1.05 / 0.9935). The P/E in
    January 2028 is taken as today's 20.82, as the card takes it. The engine has no blocks: the case enters only as VT's
    value at the move, +10.60% on its January 2028 cost before the fee.

| World shares a year (VT, before its 0.06% fee) | Stated range, central (unrounded top) | Her flexibility, 1 Jan 2033, central (plus anything above the top) | House paths with a stated top under $115,000 (shares alone, rates at forwards) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.1% (card Q124's lowest sum, with J.P. Morgan's valuation block) | $95,000 to $110,000 ($114,840) | $17,185 ($27,316) | 49.1% (51.2%) |
| 5.0% (card Q124's middle; Syzygy, 8p) | $95,000 to $115,000 ($115,811) | $18,828 ($24,959) | 46.4% (44.2%) |
| 6.5% (the median of fifteen houses' forecasts, card Q6b) | $95,000 to $115,000 ($116,602) | $20,212 ($27,159) | 44.2% (38.8%) |
| **7.5% (J.P. Morgan 2027; the register)** | **$95,000 to $115,000 ($117,142)** | **$21,179 ($28,682)** | **42.8% (35.4%)** |
| 5.0% over ten years, with the valuation fall (P/E 20.82 to about 19.5) landing before the move | $95,000 to $110,000 ($114,998, $2 under the step) | $18,224 ($28,518) | 48.7% (50.0%) |

- **What it changes in the plan: nothing in the ten payments or the bottom.** VT does not enter the completion, the
  floor or the payments' ledger; the script asserts that the completion ($39,162), the floor money ($81,244) and the
  stated bottom are identical at every return, path by path in all 200,000 house paths. The bottom is $95,000 in every row.
- **The top.** Centrally the stated top is $115,000 for any return above **3.417% a year to the move** (8p's "about
  3.4%", re-derived), and $110,000 below it. In the house paths the forecast moves the share stating under $115,000 from
  42.8% (7.5%) to 49.1% (3.1%), about 6 points; at every forecast the house median top is $115,000 and the 1-in-20 low
  $95,000 (1-in-20 high $140,000, or $135,000 at 3.1% and in the valuation case). What shares do before the move moves the
  top more than the choice of forecast does (Table 2's $95,000 to $140,000).
- **Her flexibility.** The shares kept fall with the forecast, centrally $21,179 at 7.5% to $18,828 at 5.0% and $17,185
  at 3.1% (house medians $20,486, $18,213, $16,623). With anything above the top the order is not the same: where the top
  states $5,000 lower, that $5,000 stays with her (above the top $10,130 at 3.1% against $6,131 at 5.0%), so $27,316 at
  3.1% is more than $24,959 at 5.0%.
- **Card Q124's claims, checked.**
  - *"At 5% the stated top stays $115,000 and her flexibility is about $18,828 against $21,179": holds.* The run gives
    8p's Syzygy row to the dollar; the top clears the step by $811 (by $2,142 at 7.5%). Like with like: $18,828 is set
    against $21,179, and $24,959 against $28,682 (WORDS: the second pair is "her flexibility plus anything above the top").
  - *"Only the gloomiest sum (3.1%) sits under the 3.4% hurdle": holds.* At 3.06% and 3.1% the top states $110,000 ($180
    and $160 under the step); the card's lowest sum with no valuation change, 3.70%, states $115,000, by $143. Row 23's
    arithmetic holds (+10.6% needed; yield and inflation alone give 11.9% over three years).
  - *"The step is lost mainly if valuations fall in the three years to the 2031 move": supported as far as the engine
    reaches.* The top turns on VT's value on the move day, not on the ten-year figure: the same 5.0% over ten years
    states $115,000 with its valuation fall spread over ten years and $110,000 (by $2) with the fall landed before the
    move. The case sits on the step (at a P/E of exactly 19.5 it is $7 under), so it shows that the timing carries the
    step, not a figure for prose. J.P. Morgan's 7.5% with the same fall landed before the move still states $115,000
    ($116,271; her flexibility $20,500). The engine draws VT's value whole, so whether a fall comes from valuation or from
    earnings is the card's reading (Grinold and Kroner's "noisiest component"), not a result of this run.
  - *Row 24's P/Es: corrected by about 0.1.* Compounded yearly, as the engine's growth factors are, the step goes when
    ACWI's P/E at the move falls to **20.65, 20.06, 19.39 or 19.13** (falls against earnings of 0.8%, 3.6%, 6.9% and 8.1%)
    for the card's no-valuation sums 3.70%, 4.70%, 5.90% and 6.37%. The card's 20.6, 20.0, 19.3 and 19.0 (1%, 4%, 7%, 9%)
    come back only if the sums are read as continuous rates. At a P/E of 19.5 by the move the first two state $110,000 and
    the last two $115,000 (by $99 and $333).
- **Notes for 3e (not wording).** A lower share forecast mostly lowers what Laura keeps, not what she can promise: at 5%
  a year her flexibility is about $18,800 against about $21,200, and the top she can state is the same; below about 3.4%
  a year to the move it is one $5,000 step lower; the payments and the bottom do not move. J.P. Morgan's 7.5% is "above
  the middle of published forecasts (median about 6.5%)". Never "the top stays $115,000 at every return" (8p), never that
  a forecast is wrong or too high, never $18,828 set against $28,682. "Certain" takes the stated range as its subject,
  never a figure. The valuation case's $2 and the row 24 P/Es stay here.
- **For the owner.** Card Q124: row 24 and verdict 4's P/Es read 20.7, 20.1, 19.4 and 19.1 (falls 1%, 4%, 7% and 8%) on
  the engine; its Gaps line "no run at these values" is closed by this section; rows 23 and 38 and verdict 4 hold as
  written. No figure elsewhere in this register disagrees with the run.
- **Limits.** One spread (J.P. Morgan's 17.88%) at every median; the houses' own spreads are not used. Stocks and yields
  are drawn apart in the house paths (section 7). The valuation case is one growth factor on VT with the P/E in January
  2028 set at today's 20.82 (MSCI ACWI trailing, 30 Oct 2026; VT's own 19.3 is another vendor's average, card Q124 row
  26) and the card's blocks read as yearly rates. The move on 1 Jan 2031, as everywhere in the register.

### 8w. One fund, three promises: how much of the plan rests on the late-2032 fund, IBTM (Thu 19 Nov 2026, numbers owner, MODEL and Keel arithmetic; blind judge `research/panel/2026-11-19_judge_endowment_bond_pm.md`, section 4 item 2 and section 5 item 3; `numbers/one_fund.py`, `numbers/results_one_fund_2026-11-19.json`)
- **Why.** The judge: "the plan never says plainly that one fund (IBTM) carries the 2033 payment, the bottom and the
  top, a single-instrument concentration and a single sponsor", so 3e should "make concentration explicit". Table 3's
  note says it in a parenthesis ("so one notice touches all three", `exhibits/tables.md` l.128); this block sizes it.
  New figures only; no existing figure moves.
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/one_fund.py` from the repo root (about 10 seconds).
  Imports engine, plan and completion and changes none of them; reads `results_fund_exit_2026-11-18.json` (read only,
  for part C's quotes); writes only `results_one_fund_2026-11-19.json`. It asserts every register figure it uses (floor
  money $81,244, moved money $18,349, VT $29,594 / $36,698 / $21,179, the late-2032 fund's two purchases paying $102,365

  Every assert passes.
- **Basis.** 8t's: the register as re-sized Tue 17 Nov evening, the 6 Nov 2026 curve's forwards, VT on the central 7.5%

  on 8t's headline basis it is $462,496 / $534,545 / $560,804 / $587,008.

- **What each lot pays** (section 4, 8h): the own lot $50,000 at today's forwards, $47,338 if cash waiting earns
