  $117,731, against the stated top of $115,000.
- **Promised dollars.** Of the $615,000 Laura will owe or state (the ten $50,000 payments and the $115,000 top; the
  bottom sits inside the top), **$165,000 (26.8%) rests on IBTM** from the day the shares move: the 2033 payment and
  the whole contribution. Before the move, $145,000 (the 2033 payment and the bottom). Keel arithmetic. The other nine
  payments rest on nine other holdings and the extra Treasuries; nothing else in the plan carries any part of the bottom
  or the top (part D).
- **Its size against the rest.** IBTM is the largest single holding on every date: 3.3 times the next of the ten
  payments' own holdings in January 2028 and 3.9 times from 2031 (Keel arithmetic). The short-term Treasury ETF that
  holds all waiting money is second from 2031. With IBTM's own paid distributions counted beside it, the money behind
  the promises IBTM carries is 26.1% / 29.4% / 29.3% / 29.3% of everything she holds; the part a fund event can touch is the
  bold share.
- **Never set Table 1's 32.50% against these.** 32.50% is IBTM in the WInS book at cost ($97,489.95, 8g), a $300,000
  book built before the extra Treasuries were sized; 25.7% to 25.9% is IBTM in her real plan. Two books, not a change.

**B. The fund's size, and her share of it (read at source)**
- **IBTM, iShares' page** (https://www.ishares.com/us/products/328944/ishares-ibonds-dec-2032-term-treasury-etf, read
  Thu 19 Nov 2026, Sydney): **net assets of the fund $641,351,942; shares outstanding 29,250,000**; NAV $21.9352; 15

  and $633.9m on 12 Nov (card Q151 row 13). The page's two size figures differ by 0.04% (net assets over shares
  outstanding is $21.9266 a share against the $21.9352 NAV); the percentages below use net assets. The latest annual or
  semi-annual report was not read: the page is newer.
- **Her share, real plan ($450,000), at the fund's 17 Nov 2026 size:** **0.019%** on 1 Jan 2028 ($119,141; about 5,430
  shares at that NAV), **0.022%** on 1 Jan 2031 and 1 Jan 2032 ($138,674 and $139,255; about 6,320 and 6,350 shares),
  at most 0.022% (30 Nov 2032): about 1 dollar in 4,600. Keel arithmetic, today's size against later holdings. Her
  trades are far too small to move the fund, and an early end is its board's call (card Q36), never hers.
- **For reference only, the WInS book ($300,000):** 4,503 IBTM shares (CANON D2), 0.0154% of shares outstanding.

**C. What one fund event touches at once (section 3's own figures, quoted, not recomputed; read from `results_fund_exit_2026-11-18.json` and asserted)**

An early end, a merger or a moved end of IBTM (Table 3 row 4's three events; card Q36). The range rows test exits
after the shares move; the payment row tests every month from February 2027:

| Promise on IBTM | Worst gap, cash waiting earning nothing (the standard "certain" is stated on) | Exits that fall short | At today's market rates |
|---|---|---|---|
| The top, $115,000 (both facility lots) | **$4,589 short** (exit 2 Jan 2031) | 12 of 24 monthly exits, to 1 Dec 2031; covered from 1 Jan 2032 | at least $7,295 over at every exit date ($7,459 with no exit) |
| The bottom, $95,000 (the floor lot) | **$2,887 short** (exit 2 Jan 2031) | 10 of 24, to 1 Oct 2031; covered from 1 Nov 2031 | over at every exit date |
| The 2033 payment, $50,000 (the own lot) | the ten **$1,827 short** for an exit on 1 Jan 2031 ($936 on 1 Jul 2031; $8,671 for one in February 2027), landing on the 2036 payment, where the extra Treasuries bind | exits up to 1 Jan 2032; up to 1 Jul 2032 with the 0.5% fund cost the payments are sized for ($2,756 for 1 Jan 2031) | all ten paid in every case |

- **Only a US default touches every holding behind a promise**: the ten payments' own Treasuries, the extra
  Treasuries, IBTM's three lots and the waiting money (8u: a US default is in all three promises' sets). **A fund event
  at IBTM touches only the promises IBTM carries**, the three rows above. The other nine payments' own holdings, VT and
  every distribution IBTM has already paid (part A's last money column) are outside it; her flexibility plus anything
  above the top loses at most $164 at today's market rates ($7,459 less $7,295; Keel arithmetic on section 3).
- **What it costs: two things.** The trading costs of moving the money (a fund swap), which sit in the 0.5% margin (section 0's dated line of Wed
  18 Nov evening), and the interest IBTM's bonds still had to pay, which the margin does not cover and which is met
  only from what waiting cash earns: in full at today's market rates; the top falls short only if waiting cash earns
  under 1.92% a year for the earliest exit (section 3). No probability is printed: an early end is a board's call, and
  none of the five ended iBonds Treasury funds ended a year early (section 3, "How often").

**D. Could a second fund or the Treasury bonds carry part of the bottom or the top? What the frozen IPS and book allow (CANON; no change proposed)**
- **The bottom.** IPS l.15 gives it to one fund, in the singular: "Most of the 2028 deposit will fund a Treasury fund
  maturing in late 2032, whose payout is the bare minimum you'll be able to promise your co-sponsors without doubt."
  CANON B11: TRUE (IBTM); "Whether any other WInS-listed fund ends in late 2032: UNVERIFIED." CANON D2: "WInS lists no
  Treasury maturing Feb 2031 to Feb 2036, so IBTM is the only fit for both" (the 2033 rung and the floor; TNA l.13:
  "IBTM does two jobs in Laura's plan"); in report words that is no Treasury bond, never "no Treasuries" (IBTM's own
  bonds mature in 2032).
- **The top.** IPS l.15 says only that "the other half will move into Treasuries" before the 2031 meetings, "so
  whatever figure you choose is money you already own"; it names no fund. The register books the late-2032 fund
  (`plan.py`; WORDS l.32, "the same Treasury fund the floor is"), and CANON A32 records IBTM as "the only late-2032 one
  the repo knows; the choice is UNVERIFIED". So the top rests on one fund, the late-2032 fund bought twice (January
  2028 and the move), because the plan holds no other Treasury known to pay out in late 2032.
- **The Treasury bonds.** The frozen book's five T-bonds mature 15 Feb 2037 to 15 Nov 2041 (section 0), each a 2038-2042
  payment's own holding, and the extra Treasuries' face in them belongs to the payments too. None pays out before 1 Jan
  2033, so on the contribution's date each would be worth a market price, not a figure it pays on that date. The other
  four iBond funds end December 2033 to December 2036, after the January 2033 contribution, each a payment's own holding.
- **So, within the frozen IPS and book, IBTM alone carries the bottom and the top, and it is also the 2033 payment's
  own Treasury holding.** This block states that as a fact of the plan and proposes nothing.

**Words for the plan (a note for the 27 Nov writer, not report prose; checked against WORDS, CANON and Book 19).** "One
fund carries three promises. The late-2032 Treasury fund (IBTM in Table 1) is the 2033 payment's own Treasury holding,
and the floor and the shares moved before the 2031 meetings are bought in it too, so the whole of the bottom and the top
rests on it: $165,000 of the $615,000 Laura will owe or state. Her stake in it is about 26% of everything she holds the day
the shares move, and about 1 dollar in 4,600 of the fund's $641 million. Only a US default touches every holding behind
a promise. An early
end, a merger or a moved end of this one fund touches only the promises it carries, and costs its trading costs, which
the 0.5% margin pays, and the interest its bonds still had to pay, met only from what waiting cash earns: at today's
market rates nothing is short; with cash waiting earning nothing, an end early in 2031 leaves the top up to $4,589
short." Keep "about 26%" with its day (1 Jan 2031, today's forwards; 24.8% a year later); never "about a quarter"
(WORDS l.67: the first $39,162's share of the deposit) or "about 25%" (8u: the building-cost cover); never "the fund"
or "the move" alone in report words (WORDS l.30, l.32); never "two funds" behind the top (one fund bought twice); never
"secured", "optimal" or "certain" with a figure as subject; never a probability for an early end; never Table 1's
32.50% as the plan's share (it is the WInS book's); never "barring" attached (Book 19: the event is the fund's, not the
Treasury's).

**Limits.** Central path only (today's forwards; VT on a smooth 7.5% path), so the shares are values on one path, not
a distribution. VT alone moves IBTM's share on 1 Jan 2031 to **25.1%** at section 4's house 1-in-20 low for VT that day
($18,962) and **27.0%** at its 1-in-20 high ($61,435), everything else central (`vt_sensitivity_1jan2031`; Keel
arithmetic, not a house-model distribution; yields move the floor money and the rest too). The fund's size is
today's; holders can leave as a fund nears its end (card Q151 row 13, citing card Q3c row 19), so her share of the
fund in 2032 may be larger than shown. The engine's IBTM bond stands in for the fund's holdings (section 0) and pays
twice a year, not monthly.

### 8x. The whole Taiwan-dollar series and 1986-88; "since 1991"; the tree's figures that had no register section (Sun 22 Nov 2026 evening, numbers owner; card Q217's item in section 9, parts 1-4; `numbers/SECOND_WAY_2026-11-22.md`; `numbers/twd_whole_series.py`, `numbers/results_twd_whole_series_2026-11-22.json`). Keel arithmetic on data read at source; the three-world shares in (b) and the DV01s in (e) are MODEL
- **Script and rerun:** `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/twd_whole_series.py` from the repo root (about 11
  seconds). For (b) it runs taiwan_joint.py's own code up to its fixed-stress loop, every statement that assigns to that
  script's `out` skipped (the way taiwan_joint.py runs joint_stress.py), checks that 8q's +38.9% row comes back exactly,
  then calls the same `test` with one more rise. It writes only its own results file; every results file that existed
  before is byte for byte unchanged (73 hashed before and after). Every figure below is asserted or printed by it.
- **Data, read at source Sun 22 Nov 2026 (Sydney):**
  - **FRED EXTAUS** (Board of Governors, G.5, monthly averages of noon buying rates, "Last Updated 2026-11-02"),
    https://fred.stlouisfed.org/data/EXTAUS.txt, through WebFetch and Parallel web_fetch (curl refused, 403); copied
    as read into `numbers/extaus_fred_read_2026-11-22.txt`, 517 months, Oct 1983 to Oct 2026. All 516 months of the
    repo mirror agree to the last digit (8l's check, done again); October 2026 is 32.2695, as card Q131 read it.

    Appendix 5, http://www-ws.gov.taipei/001/Upload/389/Attachment/5769321/appendix5.pdf), 1981-1993 copied into
    `numbers/taipei_cci_1981_1993_read_2026-11-22.csv`; each year's months average to the table's own yearly index

    the national "Construction Cost Indices in Taiwan Area" were compiled on trial from January 1991 and formally

**(a) The whole series (part 1).** Every two-year move on monthly averages, 8j's convention (NT$ per US$ at the start /
at the end - 1, the % more US dollars a fixed NT$ bill needs):

| Windows | Count | Largest two-year move |
|---|---|---|
| Whole FRED series, starts Oct 1983 to Oct 2024 | 493 | **+38.3%, Jan 1986 to Jan 1988** (39.5925 to 28.6278); next Dec 1985 to Dec 1987, +37.8% |
| 8j's windows (VT bought Oct 1983 to Jun 2018; moves start Oct 1986 to Jun 2021) | 417 | +29.4% (29.35%), Nov 1986 to Nov 1988 |
| Since 1991 (starts Jan 1991 to Oct 2024, card Q131's) | 406 | +16.9% (16.94%), Feb 2009 to Feb 2011 |

- So "largest +29.4%" holds for 8j's windows only (label corrected in 8j), and the series' largest is 2.26 times the
  largest since 1991 ("2.3 times", card Q217). On daily rates the same episode is +39.7% (Dec 1985 to Dec 1987, card
  Q10b, H.10 daily; not re-run). Peak to low: Aug 1985 (40.5006) to Jun 1992 (24.7695), 63.5% more US dollars.
- Table 3's 10% watch, measured from the Aug 1985 level, would have fired in **October 1986** (+10.5%); two years from
  that start the move was 33.7%, three years 41.1% (41.15%; card Q217 row 8's "41.2%" rounds it up). A trigger, never
  a size.
- Starts Oct 1983 to Dec 1990: 37 of 87 windows at 10% or more, 26 at 20% or more, 14 at 29.0% or more. Overlapping
  windows of one episode: never odds (card Q210).

**(b) Card Q217 row 7, registered (part 2).** The 1986-88 rise on her figures, by 8q's method (need = the rise times the
contribution, $115,000, in December 2032 money; left = the pot less the need, at face; 8q's central pots, unrounded):

| Rise | Need on $115,000 | Her flexibility plus anything above the top ($28,682) | Half kept alone ($21,179) | Covered with the excess (half kept alone): house / history / joint replay |

| **+38.3%, currency alone, Jan 1986 to Jan 1988** | **$44,046** | **$15,364 short** (need 1.54 times the pot) | $22,867 short | 5.8% (1.7%) / 6.7% (1.5%) / 7.5% (0.9%) |
| For comparison, 8q's worst joint rise since 1991, +38.9% | $44,756 | $16,074 short | $23,577 short | 5.2% (1.5%) / 5.8% (1.4%) / 6.8% (0.7%) |

- **Card Q217's row 7 is $1 low on each figure** ($44,045, $15,363 and $22,866): it multiplied the rise rounded to
  38.30%. The register's figures are above. On the stated bottom (the lower reading, record only, never in prose): need
  $36,386; the pot alone $7,704 short; with the $20,000 between the ends, $12,296 left.
- **What it is.** A currency-only rise, so the least the same Taiwan building cost more in US dollars over those two
  years: Taipei's building costs also rose (c). It is **not** larger than 8q's worst joint rise since 1991: 38.3% against
  38.9%, a need $710 smaller. "Larger than any since 1991" is true only of currency moves (2.3 times +16.9%) and of the
  30 strong-Taiwan-dollar windows' joint rises (highest 29.0%, (d)); never against 8q's 38.9%.

  monthly Taiwan-dollar series, which begins in October 1983, came in 1986-88: the same Taiwan-dollar bill came to cost
  38% more US dollars, from the currency alone. On her $115,000 that is about $44,000, more than her flexibility plus

**(c) Building costs in 1986-88 (part 2).** No national index exists before January 1991 (DGBAS, above), so **8q's
joint figure cannot be computed for 1986-88**. A city index exists. On Taipei City's index, Jan 1986 (39.69) to Jan 1988
(42.49) the building rose 7.1% in NT$, so with the currency the same budget cost **+48.1%** more in US dollars: a need
of $55,266 on $115,000, $26,584 beyond $28,682. The largest Taipei joint rise for starts Oct 1983 to Dec 1990 is +50.4%
(May 1987 to May 1989: costs +19.9%, the currency +25.5%); the 37 windows with the currency 10% or more stronger have a
median joint rise of 42.2%. Card Q217 rows 6 and 29 reproduce; its row 7's Taipei figures ($55,269, $26,587) are $3
high (the rise rounded to 48.06%). **A city index, not 8q's national series:** record only, or a note labelled
"Taipei City's index"; never set beside 8q's 38.9% as like for like, never the "joint rise" without its index named.

  about $5,300 ($5,267) of what Laura keeps (at today's market rates), so the price of making $5,000 certain is
  **$267, "about $270"**. "About $300" rounds it to the nearest $100, a tenth high; the register's form is "about $270".

### 8y. What a statement shows co-sponsors in 2031 and 2032 (Tue 24 Nov 2026, numbers owner's analysis worker, merged by the lead the same morning; proposal file `numbers/STATEMENT_VALUE_2026-11-24.md`; three readers playing funders, 24 Nov; `numbers/statement_value.py`, `numbers/results_statement_value_2026-11-24.json`). MODEL; Keel arithmetic where marked

- **Why.** Three readers playing funders (a Taiwanese company's head of giving, a US foundation programme officer and
  a Taiwan Ministry of Culture grants officer) said three things. Co-sponsors will be shown a broker statement at the
  2031 meetings and again before the December 2032 payout. A bond fund two years from its end shows a market value
  below what it will pay out, so the statement could show less than the $115,000 Laura signs for, and no one would be
  there to say why. And a single IBTM line mixes the 2033 payment's lot with the building's lots. This block works out
  what the statement shows. It adds new figures only; no existing figure moves.
- **Script and rerun:** run `python3 -I keel/final_report/numbers/statement_value.py` from the repo root (about 10
  seconds). It imports engine, plan and completion and changes none of them. It reads no results file and writes only
  `results_statement_value_2026-11-24.json`. It asserts every register figure it uses:
  - floor money $81,244 and moved money $18,349;
  - the floor's payout $102,365, the moved half's $20,138, and $122,503 for the two together;
  - the zero-interest values $98,096 and $117,731;
  - the unrounded ends $97,605 and $117,142, and the stated $95,000 and $115,000.

  It also reproduces these to the dollar:
  - 8w's lot values on 1 Jan 2031, 1 Jan 2032 and 30 Nov 2032, with its short-term Treasury ETF balances;
  - Exhibit 3's two yield rows ($80,000 to $100,000, paying $103,940; $110,000 to $130,000, paying $142,858);
  - section 4's top on 3.50% coupons ($117,449).

  Every assert passes.
- **Basis.** The basis is 8w's:
  - the forward rates of the 6 Nov 2026 curve;
  - VT on the central 7.5% path;
  - the escrow standard and Keel's reading of "most";
  - the move modelled on 1 Jan 2031 (the trade on Thu 2 Jan 2031).

  The terms below mean:
  - **Each IBTM lot** is valued at net asset value, as a statement shows it. That is the number of units (the money
    put in divided by that day's net asset value) times the holdings' remaining flows on the date's forward rates,
    less the 0.07% fee charged so far.
  - **A distribution the fund has already paid** waits in the short-term Treasury ETF, grown at forward rates. That
    ETF is a separate statement line, shared with every other dollar that is waiting.
  - **"The building's money on the statement"** is the two IBTM lots plus the distributions they have paid.
  - **"The count"** is what the holdings still to pay, plus the distributions already paid, hand over by 15 Dec 2032 if
    nothing earns interest from that date, after the fund's fee and the ETF's 0.15%. It is the range rule's own count,
    done again on the statement date. On each trade day it equals the rule's value (asserted).

  The exchange price is not modelled. On normal days IBTM traded 0.06% to 0.09% above net asset value (section 0).

**A. The statement on the central path**

| Statement date | The building's two IBTM lots (floor + moved), market value | Their distributions already paid, in the short-term Treasury ETF | **The building's money on the statement** | Against $115,000 | **The count** (zero interest from that date) | The 2033 payment's own IBTM lot | One IBTM line (all three lots) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Thu 2 Jan 2031 (the move) | $100,413 | $11,237 | **$111,651** | $3,349 under | **$118,644** | $38,278 | $138,691 |
| Tue 1 Jul 2031 | $100,571 | $13,657 | **$114,227** | $773 under | **$118,965** | $38,338 | $138,909 |
| Sat 3 Jan 2032 | $100,848 | $16,150 | **$116,998** | $1,998 over | **$119,363** | $38,444 | $139,291 |
| Thu 1 Jul 2032 | $101,124 | $18,703 | **$119,827** | $4,827 over | **$119,823** | $38,549 | $139,673 |
| Wed 1 Dec 2032 | $103,214 | $19,096 | **$122,310** | $7,310 over | **$122,306** | $39,357 | $142,572 |
| Wed 15 Dec 2032, the fund's end | paid out | | **$122,542** | $7,542 over | | paid out | |

- **What the building's lots pay at the end.** By this method they pay $122,542. That is $39 above section 4's
  $122,503, because plan.run charges the fund's fee on distributions after they have left the fund (8w's wedge). With
  the ETF's 0.15% charged on the waiting distributions, they pay $122,459 (section 3's no-exit figure). The top was
  set on a count of $117,731 at zero interest from each trade day: less 0.5%, that is $117,142, stated as $115,000.
- **The first date at or above $115,000, staying there (checked daily):**
  - the building's money on the statement: **Sat 23 Aug 2031** (so the first statement to show it is Mon 25 Aug);
  - the two IBTM lots alone: **never**. They reach at most $103,410, at the end;
  - the count: every day from the move. It is at least $118,644 and never under $117,731;
  - one IBTM line: every day from the move (at least $135,815 before the end). But that line is not the building's
    money (part C).
- **The 2033 payment's lot pays its own promise:** $50,000 on 1 Jan 2033 at today's forward rates, or $47,338 at zero
  interest, with the extra Treasuries paying the other $2,662 (8h). Its distributions already paid ($7,157 on
  2 Jan 2031, $10,431 on 1 Dec 2032) also wait in the short-term Treasury ETF.
- Keel arithmetic: the building's paid distributions make up 14.4% (2 Jan 2031) to 16.4% (1 Jul 2032) of the
  short-term Treasury ETF line ($78,257 and $114,382 on those dates).

**B. The Treasuries behind the building's lots: face value and the coupons still to come**

| Holdings | Face (floor lot + moved lot) | Coupons still to come, gross: 2 Jan 2031 / 1 Jul 2031 / 3 Jan 2032 / 1 Jul 2032 / 1 Dec 2032 | Count on the trade days |
|---|---|---|---|
| The engine's stand-in, which the rule uses (one bond, 4.2513%, maturing 4 Jul 2032) | $99,333 ($81,211 + $18,123) | $8,446 / $6,334 / $4,223 / $2,111 / $0 (after the fund's fee: $8,427 / $6,319 / $4,212 / $2,106 / $0) | $117,731 |
| IBTM's real 3.50% average coupon (a variant) | $102,443 ($84,057 + $18,386) | $7,171 / $5,378 / $3,586 / $1,793 / $0 | $118,039 |

- Keel arithmetic for 2 Jan 2031. The face of $99,333, plus $8,446 of coupons to come, plus $11,237 of distributions
  already paid, comes to $119,016 before the fees still to come. After the fund's fee and the ETF's 0.15% to
  15 Dec 2032, that is the count of $118,644. The market value of $111,651 is the same Treasuries priced today.
- On the 3.50% variant the IBTM lots show more ($102,073 on 2 Jan 2031, $106,053 on 1 Dec 2032), because less is
  paid out along the way. They are still under $115,000.

**C. One IBTM line, read whole.** On 2 Jan 2031 the single IBTM line shows $138,691: the building's two lots
($100,413) plus the 2033 payment's lot ($38,278). It does not include the $11,237 the building's lots have already
paid out. Read as the building's money, it is $27,041 too high on 2 Jan 2031 and $20,262 too high on 1 Dec 2032 (Keel
arithmetic). So the line is wrong in both directions: it includes money that is not the building's, and leaves out
money that is.

**D. Yields: the 1-in-20 worlds of January 2028 (Exhibit 3), and a 1-point rise during 2031**

| World | Stated range | Building lots / building money, 2 Jan 2031 | Building lots / building money, 1 Dec 2032 | Building money reaches the world's own top | Building money reaches $115,000 | Count, at least |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Central | $95,000 to $115,000 | $100,413 / $111,651 | $103,214 / $122,310 | Sat 23 Aug 2031 | Sat 23 Aug 2031 | $118,644 |
| Yields 1-in-20 low in January 2028 (1.59 points under today's forward rates; "most" binds, floor $75,001) | $80,000 to $100,000 | $88,264 / $97,718 | $88,093 / $103,838 | Mon 6 Oct 2031 | never (the lots pay $103,940) | $101,726 |
| Yields 1-in-20 high in January 2028 (1.59 points over) | $110,000 to $130,000 | $112,833 / $126,224 | $119,445 / $142,549 | Wed 25 Jun 2031 | from the move | $136,878 |

- **In every world the IBTM lots alone stay under that world's own stated top to the end.** Their peaks are $88,271
  (low), $103,410 (central) and $119,744 (high). On the move day the building's money on the statement is 2% to 3%
  under the world's top: $2,282 under $100,000 (low), $3,349 under $115,000 (central) and $3,776 under $130,000
  (high), by Keel arithmetic. That gap closes between late June and early October 2031. In the low-yield world the
  lots drift down as they near maturity ($88,264 to $86,885 by 1 Jul 2032), because their 4.25% coupon is above that
  world's rates. The money including the distributions still rises.
- **A 1-point rise all at once.** Here the short-term Treasury ETF is taken as not moving, since its bills mature
  within three months. The building's two lots fall by:
  - $1,436 (1.4%) on 2 Jan 2031;
  - $980 on 1 Jul 2031;
  - $494 on 3 Jan 2032;
  - $8 on 1 Jul 2032;
  - $0 on 1 Dec 2032, because the engine's holdings mature on 4 Jul 2032.

  A 1-point fall adds about as much ($1,458 down to $0). The 2033 payment's lot moves by $706 down to $15.
- **A 1-point rise held for good from the day after the move (2 Jan 2031).** The statement then shows:
  - $110,214 that day, against $111,651 centrally;
  - $113,315 on 1 Jul 2031, against $114,227.

  It passes $115,000 about Sun 5 Oct 2031 rather than 23 Aug. At the end the lots pay $123,351, which is $809 more,
  because the waiting money earns more. If the rise comes on 1 Jul 2031 instead, the statement shows $113,248 that
  day, passes $115,000 about Thu 9 Oct 2031, and the lots pay $123,277 at the end. The count does not fall: it is
  $118,644 on 2 Jan 2031 either way, and higher than the central path's from then on. The stated range does not move.

**What it shows**
- **A statement on the day of the move shows the building's money at about $111,700.** That is about $3,300 under
  the $115,000 she signs for. Nothing has been lost. A Treasury fund's market value is its remaining payments priced
  today, and it climbs to its payout as the fund's end nears. Centrally, the building's money passes $115,000 in late
  August 2031 and reaches about $122,300 by 1 Dec 2032. In both yield worlds the gap on the move day is also a few
  thousand dollars, closed between late June and early October 2031.
- **The IBTM lots alone never show $115,000; they reach at most about $103,400.** By December 2032 about $19,100,
  which is 16% of the building's money, has been paid out by the fund as distributions. It sits in the short-term
  Treasury ETF line, beside the payments' waiting money.
- **One IBTM line errs the other way.** It shows about $138,700 at the 2031 meetings, because it also holds the 2033
  payment's lot. Read whole, it overstates the building's money by $20,000 to $27,000.
- **Yields move the statement, not the count.** A 1-point rise the day after the move takes about $1,400 off the
  statement. The effect gets smaller each month after that, and is nothing after July 2032 on the engine's holdings.
  What the holdings hand over at zero interest does not move, and at market rates the payout goes up.

**What a co-sponsor should be shown beside the market value** (notes for the lead and the exhibits owner; not report prose)
1. **The building's money as its own figure, never the IBTM line.** That means the two lots by purchase date (a
   broker's lot detail lists each purchase with its date and cost) and the distributions they have paid.
   - Keel's suggestion, an execution choice the IPS does not touch: hold the two lots and their distributions in a
     second account at the same broker. Then one statement covers the building alone, and the 2033 payment's lot
     stays with the other nine payments.
   - In January 2028 the 2033 payment's IBTM tail ($2,286) and the floor ($81,244) are bought in the same month.
     Placing them as two orders keeps the lots apart.
2. **The count on the statement date, worked out from the fund's published holdings.** This is what these
   Treasuries, and the distributions they have already paid, hand over by mid-December 2032 if they earn nothing more
   from that day.
   - It is $118,644 on the day of the move and $122,306 by 1 Dec 2032, and never under the $117,731 the top was set on.
   - Show it with the face value of the Treasuries behind it ($99,333 on the engine's holdings, about $102,400 on
     IBTM's real coupons), the coupons still to come and the cash already paid.
   - A co-sponsor can check it against iShares' daily holdings list and her share count, without having to trust a
     forecast.
3. **One fact on why the market value is lower:** a Treasury fund's market value is what its remaining payments are
   worth today, and it rises to its payout as the fund's end nears. Beside it, give the expected statement on later
   dates at today's forward rates (the monthly series in the results file), so that the December 2032 statement
   matches what was said in 2031.
4. **The end date, and what moves each figure.** The fund ends on or about 15 Dec 2032. A rise in rates lowers the
   statement for a while but does not lower the count. An early end of the fund is the one event that would lower the
   count (section 3; 8w part C).
- **Words.** Never say "secured", "escrow" or "guaranteed" about the fund's payout. "Certain" stays the stated range's
  word and is never used for a statement figure. Never present a statement figure as a promise; say "on the central
  path". In report words, never write "the fund" alone (8w's words note; WORDS' rows for the floor and the moved
  shares).

**Limits.**
- The figures cover the central path and the two yield worlds of Exhibit 3 only, where yields move once, in January
  2028. The 1-point rise is the one stated above.
- The engine's IBTM is one bond that pays twice a year and matures on 4 Jul 2032. The real fund pays monthly and held
  notes maturing as late as 30 Nov 2032 (card Q93 row 13, iShares holdings as of 24 Sep 2026). So the real IBTM line
  falls a little each month rather than in half-year steps, and a yield move in the second half of 2032 still moves it
  slightly. The building's money (the lots plus their paid distributions) is the same either way.
- Waiting money is grown at forward rates without the ETF's 0.15% (as in 8t and 8w). With that fee charged, the
  building's money is lower by at most $83 by 15 Dec 2032.
- The exchange price can sit above or below net asset value: 0.06% to 0.09% above on normal days, and more than 1% off
  in March 2020 (section 0).
- 3 Jan 2032 is a Saturday, so a statement that day carries the close of Fri 2 Jan 2032 (under $20 different).
- Two of the daily first dates fall on weekends. The next trading day's statement is the first to show them.
- Every figure assumes the fund runs to its stated end. Section 3 and 8w part C cover an early end, a merger or a moved
  end.

### 8z. The 1994 replay, with a script (Tue 24 Nov 2026 evening, numbers owner's analysis worker, merged by the lead the same evening; card Q232 rows 11-12; `numbers/REPLAY_1994_2026-11-24.md`, `numbers/replay_1994.py`, `numbers/results_replay_1994_2026-11-24.json`, data `numbers/data/fred_1994/` with `SOURCE.txt`). MODEL; Keel arithmetic; a history replay, never a plan figure

- **Data and method.** FRED's daily constant-maturity par yields (H.15: DGS1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 20, 30), read at source
  through WebFetch on 24 Nov 2026 and identical to the repo's 27 Sep mirror; the register's curve method A
  (`engine.Curve`); 500 trading days, 31 Dec 1993 to 29 Dec 1995; both ladders held unchanged and marked daily.
- **B, a holding (lead with this one).** Ten $50,000-face Treasuries maturing 31 Dec 1999-2008, bought at par on 31 Dec
  1993 (cost $500,000; duration 7.76 years, the same as Laura's own holdings, section 1): at most 9.15% below cost on 9
  May 1994 (-9.11% on 4 Nov 1994), back to cost on 20 Apr 1995; end of 1994 -7.06%, end of 1995 +13.76%.
- **A, the ten payments' value as zeros** (card Q232 row 11's method; duration 9.97 years against the payments' 9.94):
  $272,118 on 31 Dec 1993, a high of $279,053 on 12 Jan 1994, a low of $237,959 on 4 Nov 1994 (12.6% below the start,
  14.7% below the high), back to the start value on 4 May 1995 and to the high on 9 May 1995.
- **What does not change:** $50,000 on each of the ten dates; each bond's coupons and face at maturity ($809,008 paid in
  all by B; the card's $809,058 used an exact half-year coupon grid, a $50 difference that moves no fall or date).
- **Wording.** Pair like with like: "12.6% ... back by 4 May 1995" or "14.7% ... back by 9 May 1995", never 14.7% with
  4 May (the card's row 11 mixed two starting points). Never "a ladder like hers lost about 15%": A is the value of the
  promises, which she cannot hold as zeros (STRIPS are banned). The sentence the register carries: "In 1994, ten
  $50,000-face Treasuries bought at par on 31 Dec 1993, maturing six to fifteen years later with the same 7.76-year
  duration as Laura's holdings, were worth at most 9.15% less (9 May 1994) and back to cost on 20 Apr 1995; not one
  dollar they would pay on their dates moved."

mixed, 2 not counted; all 27 scripts that assert their own figures pass, and the `numbers/` folder hashes as before.

**(a) Registered today (were in the tree or the fact sheet with no line here).**

  the extra Treasuries under the standard, 1 Jan 2027) less $300,000. The same gap is $39,162 in January 2028 money.
  Keel arithmetic. Print one of the two, with its date.

  ($287,938, Mon 5 Oct), 8b's two figures. Keel arithmetic; Section 2.5's note only, never beside the $200 of
  commissions as a ratio.
- **The 2000-04 replay, one calendar per sentence (card Q246, MSCI and DGBAS levels read at source a third time, FRED
  EXTAUS).** On Laura's calendar (31 Dec closes): world shares bought at the start of 2000 were 44.15% down at the
  start of 2003; building costs from January 2003 to December 2004 rose 18.40% in NT$ (DGBAS general index 55.05 to
  65.18) and 27.24% in US$ (the Taiwan dollar 34.5714 to 32.1703 per US$). On end-January dates: -42.68% with January
  2003 to January 2005, +17.9% NT$, +28.0% US$. Say "more than 40%"; "about 18%" and "about 27%" read the same on
  either pair. Five years on, world shares were 16.7% below cost on the price series (31 Dec 1999 to 31 Dec 2004),
  never "about 10%"; from the meetings they rose 53.8% (end-January 2003 to end-December 2004) or 49.1% on Laura's
  calendar, never "56-57%".
- **The burst's shape (card Q246):** 11.6% of the NT$ rise came in one quarter, December 2003 to March 2004, when
  rebar rose 37.8%, from world steel prices; the index had been rising since January 2002, so the burst began before
  the share low.
- **Two slumps, two bursts (card Q246 row 36):** both world-share slumps of 30% or more since 2000 were followed by a
  Taiwan building about a quarter dearer in US$: about 27% after 2000-03 (through steel) and about 24% after 2008-09
  (through the Taiwan dollar), run medians, against a typical two-year rise of about 3.0%. Dated events, never a
  frequency; the four episodes of 8x (d) are the largest rises since 1991 and only one of them followed a slump.
- **Not printed, store only:** the 4.5% February 2036 bond (CANON B21, D13: the WInS listing, never a register
  figure); "four years out of five" (card Q85); "about nine hours" before the open (card Q225); the stale prices'
  "about 1 point" (the experience record); $75 (card Q6d). Each stays on its card; a writer who uses one cites the card
  and the main session checks it there.

- Fact sheet, a waiting fund "which charges 0.15% a year": which fund is unverified; 0.15% is what the model charges

- Fact sheet, "$1,900" on 16 Oct: "about $1,900 on the measure we used that day" (version 11).
- Fact sheet, version 6's changelog line "about 42%": a changelog states what a version said; version 11 marks it

**Mon 23 Nov, morning: the 2000-2003 replay's series (sources check S4, row 50; open for the numbers owner).** MSCI's
own ACWI price levels in USD (index code 892400, read at source Mon 23 Nov) give -42.7% from end-January 2000 to
end-January 2003, -40.9% to end-December 2002 and -44.2% from end-December 1999 to end-December 2002. The register's
-42.1% ("start-of-month calendar") and -40.6% ("month-end to month-end", 8x) come from neither on its own, and may
splice in the gross series, which starts on 29 December 2000. Name the series behind each and re-run on one series
read at source. The report's "about 42%" holds on either; no figure is used beyond it until this is closed.

**Mon 23 Nov, evening: closed (the 2000-2003 replay's series; sources check S4, row 50).** Code: `numbers/twd_whole_series.py`
F4 (l.333-340), data `research/cards/data/Q85_msci_acwi_price_usd_checkpoints.csv` and `Q85_msci_acwi_gross_usd_checkpoints.csv`
(card Q85's read of 16 Nov). **What produced the old figures:** a splice. Price index to the December 2000 close, then the
GROSS-return index from 29 December 2000 (its first date), chained as price growth to Dec 2000 times gross growth after:
-42.1% = (290.11/341.63) x (258.80/379.86) - 1 (Dec 1999 close to Dec 2002 close); -40.6% = (290.11/322.83) x
(251.24/379.86) - 1 (Jan 2000 close to Jan 2003 close). Both are a price-then-gross hybrid, not one series, so neither is a
figure MSCI publishes (re-run by `numbers/replay_2000_2003_series_2026-11-23.py`, which reproduces -42.14% and -40.56%).
**Series now used:** MSCI ACWI Index, USD, PRICE (variant STRD, code 892400), end-of-month levels, read at source Mon 23 Nov 2026,
https://app2.msci.com/products/service/index/indexmaster/getLevelDataForGraph?currency_symbol=USD&index_variant=STRD&start_date=19991201&end_date=20030228&data_frequency=END_OF_MONTH&index_codes=892400
(read through the WebFetch tool; curl was blocked in this session; gross and net were not re-read, since neither is served before 29 Dec 2000).
Levels: 31 Dec 1999 341.626; 31 Jan 2000 322.834; 31 Dec 2002 190.797; 31 Jan 2003 185.033 (and 29 Dec 2000 290.113, 31 Dec 2004 284.521, card Q85).
**Results (Keel arithmetic on those levels):** end-Jan 2000 to end-Jan 2003 **-42.68%**; end-Jan 2000 to end-Dec 2002 -40.90%;
end-Dec 1999 to end-Dec 2002 -44.15%; end-Dec 1999 to end-Jan 2003 -45.84%. Price only, dividends not counted
(they would make the fall a little smaller; no gross ACWI exists at MSCI before 29 Dec 2000, so no total-return figure is
printed for these dates). Files: `numbers/results_replay_2000_2003_series_2026-11-23.json`.
**The figure the register keeps:** the replay's fall is **-42.7%, MSCI ACWI price index in US dollars, end of January 2000 to
end of January 2003** (the same month-labelled window as the building figures, which read the January 2003 index month onward).
-42.1% and -40.6% are retired. **Wording:** prose says "more than 40%" or, if a number is wanted, "about 43%" (42.68 rounds to
43; "about 42%" is 0.7 point off and is not supported by this series); with Laura's own calendar (start of January 2000 to start of
January 2003 = Dec close to Dec close) it is "about 44%". "About 42%" cannot be defended on the price series on any of the three windows
(-40.9, -42.7, -44.2), so the lead should change the words to "about 43%" or "more than 40%" (decision for the lead; the register's rule
is never to print beyond the series).
**Aha numbers:** the world-share fall belongs to aha 9 (3e's replay), not aha 3: AHA_REGISTER aha 3 is the rare year in the ten payments' cost
($287,938 on 5 Oct 2026, window from August 2007) and quotes no share fall, so aha 3 is unaffected. (CLAUDE.md's "aha 3 ... about 42%" header text
means the 2000-03 replay and should read aha 9.)
**Does the replay's other output move?** No. The building's +18.4% (NT$) and +27.2% (US$), Jan 2003 to Dec 2004, come from DGBAS and
EXTAUS (card Q131's file), not from the share series; no range end, payment or reserve figure uses the share fall (F4 feeds only the printed
sentence). Nothing re-run beyond the fall. The same row's "rising 56-57%" afterwards (AHA_REGISTER aha 9 text, from the same splice) is NOT
re-run here: price-only end-Jan 2003 to end-Dec 2004 is +53.8%; recompute the rise on the price series before printing it, or say "rose again".
